[PATCH 6.18.y 3/4] KVM: x86: Force fixed-1 bits in DR6 after synchronizing with hardware
Author: Baoli Zhang <baoli.zhang@linux.intel.com>


commit 7b88577a0983af221a78fc66c56532eb97d8fd2f upstream.

Set all fixed-1 bits in KVM's version of DR6 after synchronizing with
hardware to paper over as much of the virtualization hole as possible.
Because KVM dynamically disables DR interception, a guest can write any
DR6 bit that isn't fixed in bare metal, even if the bit is supposed to be
fixed from the guest's perspective.

In addition to providing some amount of consistency in KVM, this will allow
adding sanity checks that KVM never ends up with fixed-1 bits clear in its
version of DR6.

Link: https://patch.msgid.link/20260930193634.3415089-5-seanjc@google.com
Signed-off-by: Sean Christopherson <seanjc@google.com>
[ Baoli: 6.18 has no arch/x86/kvm/regs.h, declare kvm_get_dr6_fixed_1()
  in arch/x86/include/asm/kvm_host.h next to kvm_get_dr() instead. ]
Tested-by: Yanx L Liu <yanx.l.liu@intel.com>
Signed-off-by: Baoli Zhang <baoli.zhang@linux.intel.com>

[PATCH 6.18.y 0/4] KVM: x86: Fix DR6.BLD regression in guests
Author: Baoli Zhang <baoli.zhang@linux.intel.com>

Hi,

a58917a271cb ("KVM: x86: Preserve DR6.BLD (Bus Lock Detect) when
delivering #DB payload"), upstream 719a1d58bf43, went into 6.18.61
without the rest of its upstream series. Since then kvm-unit-tests
x86/debug fails on hosts with Bus Lock Detect when the guest CPU model
doesn't have it, e.g. the default qemu64:

  FAIL: Wanted #DB on 0x400da7 w/ DR6 = 0xffff0ff4, got 1 #DBs, addr[0] = 0x400da7, DR6 = 0xffff07f4
  ...
  SUMMARY: 25 tests, 17 unexpected failures

Once KVM stops intercepting DR accesses, the guest's write of 0 to DR6
goes to hardware and clears BLD. sync_dirty_debug_regs() copies that into
vcpu->arch.dr6, and since a58917a271cb the #DB payload path no longer
sets BLD back, so the guest sees it cleared.

Later patches in the same series fix this upstream (v7.4-rc1). This
backports those two fixes and the two cleanups they depend on. Patch 3
needed a small change: 6.18 has no arch/x86/kvm/regs.h, so the new
declaration goes in asm/kvm_host.h.

Tested on an Intel host with bus_lock_detect with a 6.18.64-based kernel
whose DR6 handling matches v6.18.64: x86/debug goes from 17 failures to
all 25 passing.

7.3.y, 6.12.y, 6.6.y and 6.1.y picked up the same commit (in 7.3.2,
6.12.117, 6.6.163 and 6.1.193) and probably need these too. I haven't
tested those, but can send backports if wanted.

Thanks,
Baoli

Sean Christopherson (4):
  KVM: x86: Rename kvm_dr6_fixed() => kvm_get_dr6_fixed_1()
  KVM: Return an "unsigned long", not "u64" for the fixed-1 DR6 bits
  KVM: x86: Force fixed-1 bits in DR6 after synchronizing with hardware
  KVM: x86: Set guest's fixed-1 DR6 bits when delivering #DB payload

[PATCH v3 3/3] KVM: selftests: Test KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY against a full dirty ring
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

Pre-fault more populated, dirty-logged pages than the vCPU's dirty ring
holds, and check that each call stops before the ring overflows, that a
retry returns ENOSPC without progress, and that each page is reported
exactly once. A read pre-fault can map a page read-only, or
access-tracked on x86 without EPT A/D bits, so skip the case if
pre-faulting doesn't dirty any page.

Acked-by: Lorenzo Stoakes (ARM) <ljs@kernel.org>
Signed-off-by: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>
---
 .../selftests/kvm/pre_fault_memory_test.c     | 102 ++++++++++++++++++
 1 file changed, 102 insertions(+)

[PATCH v3 2/3] KVM: Warn userspace against ioctls while an exit is pending
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY sets run->exit_reason to KVM_EXIT_DIRTY_RING_FULL
when it stops on a full dirty ring. If an MMIO exit is pending at that
point, the next KVM_RUN on arm64 and riscv doesn't complete it. Rather
than document that case alone, which could suggest that other ioctls
are safe, warn against invoking any ioctl other than KVM_RUN before a
pending exit is completed.

Suggested-by: Sean Christopherson <seanjc@google.com>
Signed-off-by: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>
---
 Documentation/virt/kvm/api.rst | 11 +++++++----
 1 file changed, 7 insertions(+), 4 deletions(-)

[PATCH v3 1/3] KVM: Stop KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY when the vCPU's dirty ring is full
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

Pre-faulting can map a dirty-logged page writable, which pushes it to
the vCPU's dirty ring. KVM_RUN exits with KVM_EXIT_DIRTY_RING_FULL
before the ring overflows, but the pre-fault loop never checks it, so
one call can push more entries than the ring has free, which trips the
WARN in kvm_dirty_ring_push() and overwrites entries userspace hasn't
harvested. On x86:

  WARNING: arch/x86/kvm/../../../virt/kvm/dirty_ring.c:225 at kvm_dirty_ring_push+0x7a/0xd0
  Call Trace:
   make_spte+0x2cc/0x450
   kvm_tdp_mmu_map+0x5e1/0x7f0
   kvm_tdp_page_fault+0x12c/0x1b0
   kvm_mmu_do_page_fault+0x181/0x250
   kvm_arch_vcpu_pre_fault_memory+0x136/0x1d0
   kvm_vcpu_pre_fault_memory+0xcf/0x180
   kvm_vcpu_ioctl+0x455/0x770

Stop the loop once the ring is soft full, with the check KVM_RUN makes
before entering the guest. Return the pages already processed, or
-ENOSPC when there aren't any, since a retry can't succeed until
userspace harvests and resets the ring. s390 has the ioctl but no ring,
so give kvm_dirty_ring_check_request() a stub.

The check also sets run->exit_reason to KVM_EXIT_DIRTY_RING_FULL. On
arm64, KVM_RUN completes a pending MMIO exit only if exit_reason is
still KVM_EXIT_MMIO, and on riscv KVM_RUN does the same for its MMIO,
SBI and CSR exits. If a pre-fault stops on a full ring while such an
exit is pending, the next KVM_RUN doesn't complete it and the guest
repeats the instruction that caused the exit. Userspace can avoid that
by completing the exit with KVM_RUN before pre-faulting.

Fixes: bc1a5cd002116 ("KVM: Add KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY vcpu ioctl to pre-populate guest memory")
Cc: stable@vger.kernel.org
Signed-off-by: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>
---
 Documentation/virt/kvm/api.rst | 9 +++++++--
 include/linux/kvm_dirty_ring.h | 5 +++++
 virt/kvm/kvm_main.c            | 5 +++++
 3 files changed, 17 insertions(+), 2 deletions(-)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

[PATCH v3 0/3] KVM: Bound KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY by the vCPU's dirty ring
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

Hi folks,

Changes since v2 [1]:
  - Added patch 2, which warns in api.rst against calling ioctls other
    than KVM_RUN while an exit is pending. (Sean)
  - Mentioned riscv as well as arm64 in patch 1's exit_reason
    paragraph. (Lorenzo)
  - Collected Lorenzo's Acked-by on patch 3. (thanks!)

KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY never checks the vCPU's dirty ring, so one call
over a large enough range of populated, dirty-logged pages overflows
it. The first patch stops the loop at the soft limit, as KVM_RUN does.
The second warns userspace against calling ioctls other than
KVM_RUN while an exit is pending. The third adds the case to
pre_fault_memory_test.

Pre-faulting isn't the only ioctl that can push past the ring's
reserve. On x86, KVM_SET_MSRS does too when it writes the wall clock or
the Hyper-V hypercall page. A guest can also push past the reserve
within one exit, for example through the arm64 ITS. What those paths
should do when the ring fills is an open question for the dirty ring
API, which I don't think belongs in this series.

I didn't include the kvm_dirty_ring_check_request() rewrite that Sean
sketched on v1, since I think it can lose a request. It tests the
request first and clears it afterwards. If the vCPU is scheduled out in
between, loongarch sets the PV preempted flag in guest memory and x86
updates a Xen guest's runstate area, either of which pushes an entry.
When that push makes the ring soft full, the request it makes is then
cleared, and the vCPU runs with the ring soft full until its next push.
Clearing first and re-setting, as the code does now, doesn't have that
window.

Based on Linux 7.4-rc2 (7475ea8b58616; also applies to kvm-x86/next
cbe54fc3462b3 with git am -3).

Cheers,
/fuad

[1] https://lore.kernel.org/all/20261117074428.7544-1-fuad.tabba@linux.dev/

Fuad Tabba (3):
  KVM: Stop KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY when the vCPU's dirty ring is full
  KVM: Warn userspace against ioctls while an exit is pending
  KVM: selftests: Test KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY against a full dirty ring

[PATCH v4 10/11] KVM: arm64: CCA: Support runtime faulting of memory
Author: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

At runtime, if the realm guest accesses an IPA without a stage-2
mapping, then KVM fixes up the stage-2 mapping for the backing memory.

For memory in the lower (protected) region of IPA, verify that the
corresponding guest_memfd page is private before delegating to the RMM.
The RMM then maps the delegated page into the Realm.

For memory in the upper (shared) IPA region, map the backing page as
non-secure memory. Check that a guest_memfd page is shared when the
fault uses guest_memfd; ordinary userspace-backed memslots can only be
mapped at unprotected IPAs and follow the normal user memory fault path.

If the fault IPA and guest_memfd attributes disagree then exit to
userspace with KVM_EXIT_MEMORY_FAULT so the VMM can perform the required
conversion. If a shared IPA has no memslot, KVM follows the existing
MMIO or abort-injection path. The latter can result in injecting a SEA
to the guest.

Realm faults may need to create RTTs, including when updating a
mapping after a permission fault. Take the write side of mmu_lock
and top up the MMU cache for these faults. Do not pre-fault Realm
memory.

The lock helpers are in a header compiled by nVHE too. Make
kvm_vm_hyp_is_distrusting() available there for the lock choice.

Co-developed-by: Suzuki K Poulose <suzuki.poulose@arm.com>
Signed-off-by: Suzuki K Poulose <suzuki.poulose@arm.com>
Signed-off-by: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

---
Changes since v3:
- Split Realm faults into dedicated guest_memfd and userspace
  mapping paths, leaving the native fault paths unchanged.
- Unmap earlier shared mappings if a later RMI map call fails.
- Clarify that runtime RMI data mapping does not guarantee zeroed
  contents.

Changes since v2:
- Route all guest_memfd faults through gmem_abort(). Bound each
  protected RMI map call to its encoded range, create the required
  RTT levels, and tighten retry and error handling.

Changes since v1:
- Use the write side of mmu_lock and top up the MMU cache for Realm
  faults that may create RTTs. Skip unnecessary cache flushing,
  disable Realm pre-faulting, and tighten memory-fault and mapping
  retry handling.
---

 arch/arm64/include/asm/kvm_host.h    |   4 +-
 arch/arm64/include/asm/kvm_mmu.h     |   4 +-
 arch/arm64/include/asm/kvm_rmi.h     |  12 ++

[PATCH v4 11/11] KVM: arm64: CCA: Add Realm stage-2 MMU operations
Author: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>


Register the Realm stage-2 MMU operations now that the RMM mapping
and unmapping helpers are available. Route unmapping through the
RMM, and use the Realm-specific fault handler for Realm memory faults.

The RMM handles TLB invalidation when stage-2 mappings change, so
the host has no separate TLB flush to perform. Realm stage-2 does
not support access flags, including for shared IPAs.

Realm teardown destroys non-root RTTs through RMI, so the Realm
stage-2 destruction callback frees only the root RTT pages.

Signed-off-by: Suzuki K Poulose <suzuki.poulose@arm.com>
Signed-off-by: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

---

- Register realm_mem_abort() as the Realm stage-2 fault handler.

Changes since v2:
- Move common stage-2 destruction dispatch to a separate patch;
  retain the Realm-specific destruction callback here.

[PATCH v4 09/11] KVM: arm64: CCA: Prevent memslot modifications after Realm creation
Author: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>


Once the Realm is created, prevent changes to guest_memfd backed
memslots, which can provide private memory. Validate these changes
before KVM invalidates the old memslot, so a rejected deletion leaves
its stage-2 mappings intact. Realm stage-2 faults will also validate
the private memory backing in a later commit.

Require Realm memslots to lie in the lower half of the IPA space, as the
upper, shared half aliases the lower, protected half.

Define kvm_arch_has_private_mem() so generic KVM recognizes Realm VMs
as supporting private memory. Defining the macro also makes
gmem_in_place_conversion true for every arm64 VM, so guest_memfd
memslots are marked gmem-only even for non-Realm VMs. A well-formed
KVM_SET_MEMORY_ATTRIBUTES2 request on a non-Realm guest_memfd now
reaches the VM check and returns -EINVAL instead of -ENOTTY.

Additionally, similar to pKVM, do not support dirty logging or
read-only memslots.

Signed-off-by: Suzuki K Poulose <suzuki.poulose@arm.com>
Signed-off-by: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

---
Changes since v3:
- Explain the arm64-wide in-place conversion effect and keep the
  general IPA limit comment.

Changes since v1:
- Preserve private mappings if KVM invalidates a Realm guest_memfd
  slot before the prepare hook rejects its deletion.
---

[PATCH v4 08/11] KVM: Allow architectures to reject memslot changes before invalidation
Author: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>


The next patch needs to reject changes to a realm guest_memfd memslot
before invalidation. KVM currently invalidates the slot and calls
kvm_arch_flush_shadow_memslot() before kvm_arch_prepare_memory_region()
can reject the request. For a realm, invalidation unmaps stage-2
mappings; restoring the old memslot on failure does not restore those
mappings. Thus a rejected deletion or move would still have side effects.

Add kvm_arch_validate_memory_region() and invoke it before invalidating
the slot, with slots_lock and slots_arch_lock held. The hook performs
validation only, allowing architectures to reject a request without
changing the memslot or its mappings. Provide a weak no-op implementation
for architectures that do not need early validation.

Move arm64's existing pVM checks into the new hook as part of early
validation. pVM invalidation uses no_stage2_unmap_range(), so the realm
case motivates this hook. Keep resource preparation after invalidation,
since invalidation drops slots_arch_lock and allows concurrent updates
to architecture-specific memslot metadata.

Signed-off-by: Suzuki K Poulose <suzuki.poulose@arm.com>
Signed-off-by: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

---
Changes since v3:
- Clarify that Realm stage-2 invalidation motivates the early
  validation hook; pVM invalidation does not unmap stage-2.
---

 arch/arm64/kvm/pkvm.c    |  2 +-
 include/linux/kvm_host.h |  4 ++++
 virt/kvm/kvm_main.c      | 33 ++++++++++++++++++++++++++-------
 4 files changed, 47 insertions(+), 14 deletions(-)

[PATCH v4 06/11] KVM: arm64: CCA: Destroy RTTs
Author: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

The RMM owns the stage 2 page tables for a realm, and KVM must request
that the RMM creates/destroys entries as necessary. The physical pages
to store the page tables are delegated to the realm as required, and can
be undelegated when no longer used.

Creating new RTTs is the easy part, destroying them is a little more
tricky. The result of realm_rtt_destroy() can be used to effectively
walk the tree and destroy the entries (undelegating pages that were
given to the realm).

Tear down a created Realm whether it is still new or has been activated.
Unmap the Realm IPA space once during stage-2 teardown. Defer
destruction of non-root RTTs until final Realm destruction, after
REALM_TERMINATE and before destroying the RD; root RTTs remain until the
RD has gone away.

Use the same minimum of two stage-2 levels as VTCR when deriving
the Realm's starting RTT level. Without this, narrow IPAs on
64K-page hosts can give the RMM a different starting level from
KVM's own page tables.

Signed-off-by: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

---
Changes since v3:
- Match VTCR's minimum of two stage-2 levels for Realm RTTs.
- Use explicit config_lock cleanup in Realm destruction.

Changes since v2:
- Use the actual stage-2 start level and correct the RTT skip
  granularity. Free SRO state on final teardown errors.

Changes since v1:
- Tear down created Realms in both NEW and ACTIVE states. Fix the
  RTT start level, page accounting on failed undelegation, and
  handling of RTT granules returned by RMI.
---
 arch/arm64/include/asm/kvm_rmi.h |   3 +
 arch/arm64/kvm/mmu.c             |  17 ++-
 arch/arm64/kvm/rmi.c             | 178 +++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++
 3 files changed, 197 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-)

[PATCH v4 01/11] KVM: arm64: guest_memfd: Check mmap support when preparing memslot
Author: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

Arm64 requires mmap-capable guest_memfd backing because the memslot HVA
may still be used to access shared memory even when guest faults use
guest_memfd.

The existing check rejects guest_memfd without mmap support based on the
memslot's gmem-only flag. That stops working when arm64 defines
kvm_arch_has_private_mem() for realms: in-place conversion then marks all
guest_memfd memslots gmem-only, including those backed by files without
GUEST_MEMFD_FLAG_MMAP. Such files would pass the existing check.

Check the backing file's mmap capability directly to keep requiring it
for every arm64 VM. Expose a helper to reuse kvm_gmem_supports_mmap().

Signed-off-by: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

---
Changes since v3:
- Explain why in-place conversion makes checking the memslot's
  gmem-only flag insufficient for arm64.

Changes since v2:
- Require mmap-capable guest_memfd backing for all arm64 VMs, as
  shared-memory access can use the memslot HVA.

Changes since v1:
- New patch in v2. Check the guest_memfd backing file rather than the
  memslot flag when deciding whether arm64 can map the backing memory.
---

[PATCH v4 00/11] KVM: arm64: CCA: Prepare Realm memory management
Author: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

This series adds the memory management plumbing for CCA, building on the
basic RMM support [1]. Thanks to everyone who reviewed and tested the
earlier versions.

The patches introduce internal Realm VM state, prepare Realm stage-2
setup and teardown, and provide RTT mapping and invalidation helpers.
Realm memslots are restricted to the lower half of the IPA space because
the protected and shared halves alias each other. The final patch
connects the helpers to KVM's stage-2 MMU operations.

This is the memory management portion of the larger Realm host series.
Realm descriptor creation, REC entry and exit, activation, and the new
uAPI follow in the integration stack. This series alone does not enable
Realm creation.

The integration branch [2] includes Realm creation, REC entry and exit,
and the remaining host support on top of this series. The MM branch [3]
contains just this series.

Changes since v3:
- Keep pKVM-specific protected VM handling in the nVHE path and move
  stage-2 destruction dispatch before basic Realm infrastructure.
- Derive the Realm RTT start level from VTCR, matching KVM's minimum
  two-level stage-2 layout for narrow IPAs on 64K-page hosts.
- Split Realm guest_memfd and userspace-backed faults into dedicated
  paths, leaving the native fault handlers unchanged.
- Unmap earlier shared mappings when a later RMI map call fails.
- Clarify the early memslot validation rationale and the arm64-wide
  effect of in-place guest_memfd conversion.

Changes since v2:
- Move RMM mode reservation and RMI availability and feature checks to
  the integration stack.
- Require mmap-capable guest_memfd backing for all arm64 VMs and route
  all guest_memfd faults through gmem_abort().
- Store the Realm Descriptor physical address from initial Realm setup,
  and improve SRO cleanup and RTT teardown error handling.
- Use a local MMU cache for RTT invalidation, handle failed splits, and
  validate returned RTT levels.
- Bound protected RMI mapping calls to the encoded range and tighten
  RTT creation, retry, and error handling.
- Split common stage-2 destruction dispatch into its own patch before
  registering the Realm stage-2 MMU operations.

Changes since v1:
- Add a guest_memfd prerequisite patch that checks whether the backing
  file supports mmap when preparing an arm64 memslot.
- Combine the RMI availability and feature checks into one patch, and
  move Realm vCPU feature restrictions to the integration stack.
- Correct Realm IPA sizing and RTT teardown, including teardown before
  activation, RTT accounting, and handling of missing or folded RTTs.
- Rework RTT mapping to respect IPA and physical alignment, handle
  partial RMI progress, and retry interrupted operations.
- Preserve private mappings when a Realm guest_memfd memslot deletion is
  rejected, and tighten Realm fault handling and stage-2 destruction.

[PATCH v7 4/4] powerpc/powernv/eeh: Map generic EEH error types to OPAL
Author: Narayana Murty N <nnmlinux@linux.ibm.com>

eeh_pe_inject_err() is called with generic EEH error types defined in
the VFIO/EEH userspace ABI (EEH_ERR_TYPE_32, EEH_ERR_TYPE_64). The
PowerNV backend previously compared these generic values directly against
OPAL firmware constants (OPAL_ERR_INJECT_TYPE_IOA_BUS_ERR and
OPAL_ERR_INJECT_TYPE_IOA_BUS_ERR64).

This is fragile: it relies on the generic EEH ABI values being
numerically identical to OPAL-specific values, and would silently
misbehave if they ever diverged.

The preceding patches introduce RTAS-based error injection on pSeries,
mapping generic EEH error types to PAPR error-injection classes. Apply
the same explicit translation in the PowerNV backend, which uses OPAL
instead of RTAS.

Add an explicit mapping in pnv_eeh_err_inject() that translates:
EEH_ERR_TYPE_32 -> OPAL_ERR_INJECT_TYPE_IOA_BUS_ERR
EEH_ERR_TYPE_64 -> OPAL_ERR_INJECT_TYPE_IOA_BUS_ERR64

Return -ERANGE for unsupported error types, preserving the behavior
of the original code. This decouples the VFIO/EEH interface from OPAL
implementation details.

No change to the existing userspace ABI or PowerNV behavior.

Signed-off-by: Narayana Murty N <nnmlinux@linux.ibm.com>
---
 arch/powerpc/platforms/powernv/eeh-powernv.c | 22 +++++++++++++++-----
 1 file changed, 17 insertions(+), 5 deletions(-)

[PATCH v7 2/4] powerpc/pseries/eeh: Implement RTAS-based EEH error injection
Author: Narayana Murty N <nnmlinux@linux.ibm.com>

pseries_eeh_err_inject() only emulates MMIO errors in software and
ignores the address and mask.  Use the PAPR RTAS services
ibm,open-errinjct, ibm,errinjct and ibm,close-errinjct instead, so the
platform raises the error for a given IOA bus address.

EEH_ERR_TYPE_32 and EEH_ERR_TYPE_64 select the ioa-bus-error and
ioa-bus-error-64 classes and the matching parameter buffer layout.  The
class token is read from the "ibm,errinjct-tokens" property of /rtas; if
the class is not advertised the request fails with -EOPNOTSUPP.  The
buffer comes from the RTAS work area allocator, and the session is closed
on every exit path after a successful open.  A mutex serializes sessions
started through this backend.  Statuses specific to these services, such
as "already open", are mapped to errno locally; rtas_error_rc() is
unchanged.

For memory and I/O load/store functions the address must be a PCI bus
address within an assigned standard BAR (BAR0-BAR5) of the matching type,
memory or I/O, of a device in the PE.  pci_lock_rescan_remove() protects
the PE device list walk and snapshots the PE address and BUID before
releasing the lock and opening the RTAS session.  Config-space and DMA
functions, and requests with address and mask both zero, are not checked.
The mask is passed to firmware unchanged.

Remove eeh_pe_inject_mmio_error().  eeh_break_device() is now used only by
debugfs, so build it only with CONFIG_DEBUG_FS.

Firmware without the ERRINJCT services or class token no longer gets the
software-emulated injection; the request fails with -ENODEV or
-EOPNOTSUPP.

Signed-off-by: Narayana Murty N <nnmlinux@linux.ibm.com>
---
 arch/powerpc/include/asm/eeh.h               |  26 +-
 arch/powerpc/kernel/eeh.c                    |  10 +-
 arch/powerpc/platforms/pseries/eeh_pseries.c | 334 +++++++++++++++++--
 3 files changed, 338 insertions(+), 32 deletions(-)

[PATCH v7 1/4] powerpc/rtas: Handle ibm,open-errinjct return format
Author: Narayana Murty N <nnmlinux@linux.ibm.com>

ibm,open-errinjct returns the session token in rets[0] and the status in
rets[1], unlike other RTAS functions, which return the status in rets[0].
rtas_call() would report the session token as the status and drop the
real status.

Take the status from rets[1] for this function and return the session
token in outputs[0].  This needs exactly two return cells, so reject any
other count from kernel callers with -EINVAL and a one-time warning, and
document the exception in the rtas_call() kernel-doc.

Give the rtas_output tracepoint the index of the status cell, so that it
reports rets[1] as the status and the session token as an additional
output for this function.  A call with no return cells reports status 0
instead of reading a cell that does not exist.

Ensure __fetch_rtas_last_error() and sys_rtas() initialize rtas_args.rets
to point into rtas_args.args[] after copying local arguments, preventing
tracepoint handlers from dereferencing uninitialized memory or reading
stale on-stack values.

sys_rtas() still copies the raw return cells to userspace.  It uses the
status from the right cell for hardware-error (-1) detection and, after
the lockdown check, rejects fewer than two return cells for this function.

Link: https://files.openpower.foundation/s/XFgfMaqLMD5Bcm8
Signed-off-by: Narayana Murty N <nnmlinux@linux.ibm.com>
---
 arch/powerpc/include/asm/trace.h | 26 +++++++---
 arch/powerpc/kernel/rtas.c       | 83 ++++++++++++++++++++++++++------
 2 files changed, 88 insertions(+), 21 deletions(-)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

[PATCH v7 3/4] vfio/spapr_tce: Normalize EEH IOA error injection addresses
Author: Narayana Murty N <nnmlinux@linux.ibm.com>

On pseries, RTAS error injection takes a PCI bus address for memory and
I/O load/store functions.  VFIO_EEH_PE_INJECT_ERR callers such as QEMU
pass an address within a BAR as the sysfs "resource" file exports it: a
CPU address for a memory BAR and a PCI bus address for an I/O BAR.  A
CPU address can differ from the PCI bus address because of PHB window
translation.

On pseries, convert the address before calling eeh_pe_inject_err() to the
PCI bus address of the matching standard BAR (BAR0-BAR5) of a device in
the IOMMU group, keeping its offset.  Memory functions match memory BARs
and I/O functions match I/O BARs.  Unassigned and disabled BARs are never
matched, and an address that matches no BAR fails with -EINVAL.

Config-space and DMA functions, requests with address and mask both zero,
and requests on other platforms are not changed.  The mask is never
modified.

Signed-off-by: Narayana Murty N <nnmlinux@linux.ibm.com>
---
 drivers/vfio/vfio_iommu_spapr_tce.c | 131 +++++++++++++++++++++++++++-
 1 file changed, 130 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-)

[PATCH v7 0/4] powerpc: Add RTAS based error injection support for pSeries
Author: Narayana Murty N <nnmlinux@linux.ibm.com>

The pSeries EEH error-injection backend currently implements a limited
software-generated MMIO failure and does not use the error-injection
services provided by RTAS.

This series replaces that implementation with the PAPR-defined RTAS
workflow based on:

- ibm,open-errinjct
- ibm,errinjct
- ibm,close-errinjct

The implementation opens an RTAS error-injection session, allocates and
prepares an RTAS-accessible work area, performs the requested injection,
releases the work area, and closes the session on both success and failure
paths.

The existing EEH userspace ABI is preserved. EEH_ERR_TYPE_32 and
EEH_ERR_TYPE_64 continue to represent generic 32-bit and 64-bit IOA
bus-error injection requests. The pSeries backend maps these values to
the corresponding RTAS error types, while the PowerNV backend explicitly
maps them to the corresponding OPAL types.

Additional generic EEH error types (EEH_ERR_TYPE_RECOVERED_SPECIAL_EVENT,
EEH_ERR_TYPE_CORRUPTED_PAGE, and the cache/TLB corruption types) are
defined in the UAPI header and mapped explicitly to RTAS firmware
encodings by the pSeries backend. Platform backends that do not support
a valid generic type return -EOPNOTSUPP.

No existing userspace ABI values are changed.

The injection path can be exercised for VFIO-assigned devices through
VFIO_EEH_PE_INJECT_ERR. The guest or userspace VFIO application continues
to use the same generic EEH type and function values, independent of
whether the host platform uses RTAS or OPAL.

For IOA bus-error injection, the VFIO sPAPR path normalizes a Linux BAR
resource address to the corresponding PCI bus address before dispatching
the request to the platform EEH backend. Address normalization is
restricted to memory and IO load/store functions whose addr argument is a
BAR-backed PCI address. Config-space and DMA functions are passed through
without translation because their addr arguments represent PCI config
register offsets and IOVA/TCE addresses respectively. The no-address form
is represented by both address and mask being zero.

The series also handles the unusual return format of ibm,open-errinjct:

rets[0] = error-injection session token
rets[1] = RTAS status

rtas_call() returns rets[1] as the status and places the session token in
outputs[0], preserving the normal kernel rtas_call() convention.

sys_rtas() continues to expose the raw RTAS return cells to userspace.
For ibm,open-errinjct, it validates that at least two return cells were
requested before entering RTAS and uses the RTAS status cell for
last-error handling. Userspace therefore continues to receive the session
token and status in their PAPR-defined positions.

The pSeries backend uses the existing RTAS work-area allocator for the
error-injection parameter buffer. The work area is allocated only after
ibm,open-errinjct succeeds and is released before ibm,close-errinjct.
RTAS work areas reside below RTAS_INSTANTIATE_MAX, so the physical address
fits in the 32-bit RTAS argument passed to firmware.

The complete open, inject, and close sequence is serialized with a mutex.
RTAS busy and extended-delay return values are handled for all three
calls. Once a session has been opened, ibm,close-errinjct is attempted
even when parameter preparation or error injection fails.

The patches are organized as follows:

1. Handle the special ibm,open-errinjct return format in rtas_call().
2. Add pSeries RTAS error-injection validation and buffer helpers.
3. Implement RTAS-based pSeries EEH error injection.
4. Normalize VFIO sPAPR IOA error-injection addresses.
5. Explicitly map generic EEH error types to OPAL types on PowerNV.

Testing was performed on PowerVM with firmware providing the RTAS
error-injection services and with corresponding QEMU support.

The corresponding QEMU v6 series is posted here:
https://lore.kernel.org/qemu-devel/20261019162541.68440-1-nnmlinux@linux.ibm.com/

Signed-off-by: Narayana Murty N <nnmlinux@linux.ibm.com>
---

    the pSeries error-injection implementation.

    whose addr argument is a BAR-backed MMIO/IO address. Guard

    predicate so config offsets and DMA/IOVA addresses bypass BAR
    membership validation.

    pr_err() to pr_debug() since invalid user-supplied inputs do not
    represent internal kernel errors.
  - Update prepare_errinjct_buffer() kernel-doc comments to document
    function-specific addr semantics (MEM/IO = BAR, CFG = offset, DMA = IOVA).

* Patch 4:
  - Restrict address normalization to error functions carrying BAR-backed
    PCI MMIO/IO addresses (memory and IO load/store). Config-space and
    DMA functions bypass normalization rather than incorrectly returning
    -EINVAL.
  - Fix false matches against unassigned/disabled BARs in

    IORESOURCE_UNSET / IORESOURCE_DISABLED.

  - Update commit description to accurately reflect function-specific
    address domains.

  - No changes.

[PATCH 2/2] KVM: LoongArch: selftests: Free the VM when the PMU test skips
Author: Gokul K <gokul02k@gmail.com>

main() creates the VM before asking whether it has the PMU feature, and
when it does not, returns KSFT_SKIP directly. That skips the done: label
through which every other exit frees the VM. Set ret and jump there
instead.

[PATCH 1/2] MAINTAINERS: Add the LoongArch KVM selftests directory
Author: Gokul K <gokul02k@gmail.com>

The KVM/LoongArch entry covers tools/testing/selftests/kvm/*/loongarch/
and lib/loongarch/, but not tools/testing/selftests/kvm/loongarch/,
where the tests themselves live. The */loongarch/ pattern needs a
directory between kvm/ and loongarch/, so it does not match it.

As a result get_maintainer.pl omits the LoongArch KVM maintainers and
loongarch@lists.linux.dev for patches to those tests. arm64, riscv, s390
and x86 each list both forms.

Signed-off-by: Gokul K <gokul02k@gmail.com>

[PATCH v7 4/4] powerpc/powernv/eeh: Map generic EEH error types to OPAL
Author: Narayana Murty N <nnmlinux@linux.ibm.com>

eeh_pe_inject_err() is called with generic EEH error types defined in
the VFIO/EEH userspace ABI (EEH_ERR_TYPE_32, EEH_ERR_TYPE_64). The
PowerNV backend previously compared these generic values directly against
OPAL firmware constants (OPAL_ERR_INJECT_TYPE_IOA_BUS_ERR and
OPAL_ERR_INJECT_TYPE_IOA_BUS_ERR64).

This is fragile: it relies on the generic EEH ABI values being
numerically identical to OPAL-specific values, and would silently
misbehave if they ever diverged.

The preceding patches introduce RTAS-based error injection on pSeries,
mapping generic EEH error types to PAPR error-injection classes. Apply
the same explicit translation in the PowerNV backend, which uses OPAL
instead of RTAS.

Add an explicit mapping in pnv_eeh_err_inject() that translates:
EEH_ERR_TYPE_32 -> OPAL_ERR_INJECT_TYPE_IOA_BUS_ERR
EEH_ERR_TYPE_64 -> OPAL_ERR_INJECT_TYPE_IOA_BUS_ERR64

Return -ERANGE for unsupported error types, preserving the behavior
of the original code. This decouples the VFIO/EEH interface from OPAL
implementation details.

No change to the existing userspace ABI or PowerNV behavior.

Signed-off-by: Narayana Murty N <nnmlinux@linux.ibm.com>

 1 file changed, 17 insertions(+), 5 deletions(-)

[PATCH v7 0/4] powerpc: Add RTAS based error injection support for pSeries

error-injection services and with corresponding QEMU support.

The corresponding QEMU v6 series is posted here:
https://lore.kernel.org/qemu-devel/20261019162541.68440-1-nnmlinux@linux.ibm.com/

Signed-off-by: Narayana Murty N <nnmlinux@linux.ibm.com>

V5 -> V6:
https://lore.kernel.org/all/20261019101813.45085-1-nnmlinux@linux.ibm.com/
* Addressed v5 review comments from Sashiko.

* Patch 1:
  - No changes.

* Patch 2:
  - Fix false BAR matches against unassigned or disabled BARs in
    validate_addr_mask_in_pe(): use pci_resource_len() instead of
    resource_size() (which returns 1 for zero-initialized BARs), and
    explicitly reject BARs with IORESOURCE_UNSET or IORESOURCE_DISABLED.
  - Move validate_errinjct_args() and prepare_errinjct_buffer() to
    Patch 3 alongside their callers so each intermediate commit builds
    cleanly with CONFIG_WERROR=y without unused-function warnings.

* Patch 3:
  - Include <asm/rtas-work-area.h> for the RTAS work-area API used by
    the pSeries error-injection implementation.
  - Introduce validate_errinjct_args() and prepare_errinjct_buffer() here
    alongside their callers (moved from Patch 2).
  - Fix eeh_break_device() visibility: keep it under #ifdef CONFIG_DEBUG_FS
    to prevent unused-function compiler warnings when CONFIG_DEBUG_FS=n.
  - Add pseries_eeh_func_needs_bar_addr() to identify error functions
    whose addr argument is a BAR-backed MMIO/IO address. Guard
    validate_addr_mask_in_pe() in prepare_errinjct_buffer() with this
    predicate so config offsets and DMA/IOVA addresses bypass BAR
    membership validation.
  - Demote the validate_addr_mask_in_pe() diagnostic message from
    pr_err() to pr_debug() since invalid user-supplied inputs do not
    represent internal kernel errors.
  - Update prepare_errinjct_buffer() kernel-doc comments to document
    function-specific addr semantics (MEM/IO = BAR, CFG = offset, DMA = IOVA).

* Patch 4:
  - Restrict address normalization to error functions carrying BAR-backed
    PCI MMIO/IO addresses (memory and IO load/store). Config-space and
    DMA functions bypass normalization rather than incorrectly returning
    -EINVAL.
  - Fix false matches against unassigned/disabled BARs in
    vfio_spapr_eeh_normalize_addr() using pci_resource_len() and checking
    IORESOURCE_UNSET / IORESOURCE_DISABLED.
  - Remove the misleading ", injection may fail" suffix from the
    pr_debug() message since the caller returns -EINVAL immediately.
  - Update commit description to accurately reflect function-specific
    address domains.

* Patch 5:
  - No changes.

[PATCH v7 2/4] powerpc/pseries/eeh: Implement RTAS-based EEH error injection
Author: Narayana Murty N <nnmlinux@linux.ibm.com>

pseries_eeh_err_inject() only emulates MMIO errors in software and
ignores the address and mask.  Use the PAPR RTAS services
ibm,open-errinjct, ibm,errinjct and ibm,close-errinjct instead, so the
platform raises the error for a given IOA bus address.

EEH_ERR_TYPE_32 and EEH_ERR_TYPE_64 select the ioa-bus-error and
ioa-bus-error-64 classes and the matching parameter buffer layout.  The
class token is read from the "ibm,errinjct-tokens" property of /rtas; if
the class is not advertised the request fails with -EOPNOTSUPP.  The
buffer comes from the RTAS work area allocator, and the session is closed
on every exit path after a successful open.  A mutex serializes sessions
started through this backend.  Statuses specific to these services, such
as "already open", are mapped to errno locally; rtas_error_rc() is
unchanged.

For memory and I/O load/store functions the address must be a PCI bus
address within an assigned standard BAR (BAR0-BAR5) of the matching type,
memory or I/O, of a device in the PE.  pci_lock_rescan_remove() protects
the PE device list walk and snapshots the PE address and BUID before
releasing the lock and opening the RTAS session.  Config-space and DMA
functions, and requests with address and mask both zero, are not checked.
The mask is passed to firmware unchanged.

Remove eeh_pe_inject_mmio_error().  eeh_break_device() is now used only by
debugfs, so build it only with CONFIG_DEBUG_FS.

Firmware without the ERRINJCT services or class token no longer gets the
software-emulated injection; the request fails with -ENODEV or
-EOPNOTSUPP.

Signed-off-by: Narayana Murty N <nnmlinux@linux.ibm.com>
---
 arch/powerpc/include/asm/eeh.h               |  26 +-
 arch/powerpc/kernel/eeh.c                    |  10 +-
 arch/powerpc/platforms/pseries/eeh_pseries.c | 334 +++++++++++++++++--
 3 files changed, 338 insertions(+), 32 deletions(-)

[PATCH v7 3/4] vfio/spapr_tce: Normalize EEH IOA error injection addresses
Author: Narayana Murty N <nnmlinux@linux.ibm.com>

On pseries, RTAS error injection takes a PCI bus address for memory and
I/O load/store functions.  VFIO_EEH_PE_INJECT_ERR callers such as QEMU
pass an address within a BAR as the sysfs "resource" file exports it: a
CPU address for a memory BAR and a PCI bus address for an I/O BAR.  A
CPU address can differ from the PCI bus address because of PHB window
translation.

On pseries, convert the address before calling eeh_pe_inject_err() to the
PCI bus address of the matching standard BAR (BAR0-BAR5) of a device in
the IOMMU group, keeping its offset.  Memory functions match memory BARs
and I/O functions match I/O BARs.  Unassigned and disabled BARs are never
matched, and an address that matches no BAR fails with -EINVAL.

Config-space and DMA functions, requests with address and mask both zero,
and requests on other platforms are not changed.  The mask is never
modified.

Signed-off-by: Narayana Murty N <nnmlinux@linux.ibm.com>
---
 drivers/vfio/vfio_iommu_spapr_tce.c | 131 +++++++++++++++++++++++++++-
 1 file changed, 130 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-)

[PATCH v7 1/4] powerpc/rtas: Handle ibm,open-errinjct return format
Author: Narayana Murty N <nnmlinux@linux.ibm.com>

ibm,open-errinjct returns the session token in rets[0] and the status in
rets[1], unlike other RTAS functions, which return the status in rets[0].
rtas_call() would report the session token as the status and drop the
real status.

Take the status from rets[1] for this function and return the session
token in outputs[0].  This needs exactly two return cells, so reject any
other count from kernel callers with -EINVAL and a one-time warning, and
document the exception in the rtas_call() kernel-doc.

Give the rtas_output tracepoint the index of the status cell, so that it
reports rets[1] as the status and the session token as an additional
output for this function.  A call with no return cells reports status 0
instead of reading a cell that does not exist.

Ensure __fetch_rtas_last_error() and sys_rtas() initialize rtas_args.rets
to point into rtas_args.args[] after copying local arguments, preventing
tracepoint handlers from dereferencing uninitialized memory or reading
stale on-stack values.

sys_rtas() still copies the raw return cells to userspace.  It uses the
status from the right cell for hardware-error (-1) detection and, after
the lockdown check, rejects fewer than two return cells for this function.

Link: https://files.openpower.foundation/s/XFgfMaqLMD5Bcm8
Signed-off-by: Narayana Murty N <nnmlinux@linux.ibm.com>
---
 arch/powerpc/include/asm/trace.h | 26 +++++++---
 arch/powerpc/kernel/rtas.c       | 83 ++++++++++++++++++++++++++------
 2 files changed, 88 insertions(+), 21 deletions(-)

[PATCH v1 10/10] PCI/TSM: Configure accepted device DMA during driver attachment
Author: Aneesh Kumar K.V (Arm) <aneesh.kumar@kernel.org>

Device acceptance and DMA enablement are separate operations. An
accepted device may still need TSM-specific setup before it can perform
DMA.

Add dma_enable and dma_disable callbacks for device-security TSMs. For
TSMs implementing these callbacks, require the device to be accepted
before configuring DMA. Reject drivers setting driver_managed_dma, which
are not supported by this patch.

Take a TSM user reference before DMA configuration and retain it until
DMA cleanup finishes. This also references the TSM device and its driver
module, so tsm_unregister() waits for cleanup and the callback code
remains available. Record whether DMA setup holds this reference in
pci_dev::tsm_dma_pinned.

Signed-off-by: Aneesh Kumar K.V (Arm) <aneesh.kumar@kernel.org>

[PATCH v1 09/10] PCI/TSM: Build encrypted MMIO descriptors from a supplied report
Author: Aneesh Kumar K.V (Arm) <aneesh.kumar@kernel.org>

The TDISP Device Interface Report describes a device interface's
configuration, including its MMIO ranges and their attributes. Each MMIO
range identifies its BAR, starting physical address and size.

The device adds the MMIO Reporting Offset (MRO) to the physical
addresses included in the report. When the MRO is a multiple of the BAR
size, masking the reported address by the BAR size recovers the offset
within that BAR. Combining this offset with the guest's PCI resource
start gives the corresponding guest physical address.

TSM drivers need to parse these reports and identify the MMIO ranges
requiring private mappings. Provide common PCI/TSM helpers so each
driver does not have to implement the same parsing and resource setup.

Add pci_tsm_mmio_alloc_report() to parse a caller-supplied report.
Record each range's guest physical address and retain its reported
address for firmware mapping validation. Skip non-TEE and currently
unsupported MSI-X ranges.

Add helpers to register the ranges in iomem with IORES_DESC_ENCRYPTED,
Setup requires device::mutex and rejects devices with a PCI driver
attached.

A later guest attestation patch uses these helpers during
evidence-accept. The caller remains responsible for verifying the report
and validating the MMIO mappings before allowing device use.

Based on the original patch from Dan Williams <djbw@kernel.org>
Signed-off-by: Aneesh Kumar K.V (Arm) <aneesh.kumar@kernel.org>
---
 drivers/pci/tsm.c       | 227 ++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++
 include/linux/ioport.h  |   1 +
 include/linux/pci-tsm.h |  35 +++++++
 3 files changed, 263 insertions(+)

[PATCH v1 08/10] PCI/TSM: Add sysfs lock and unlock for guest-assigned devices
Author: Aneesh Kumar K.V (Arm) <aneesh.kumar@kernel.org>

A confidential guest needs to lock a PCI device before collecting and
verifying evidence about it. Userspace also needs a way to unlock the
device after use or when verification fails.

Add tsm/lock and tsm/unlock sysfs attributes for TSM drivers
implementing devsec_ops. Writing a TSM device name to tsm/lock calls its
lock callback. Unlock calls the matching driver's unlock callback

Take device::mutex to prevent driver attachment, then pci_tsm_rwsem
for write to protect pci_dev::tsm during either operation. This order
matches PCI DMA preparation, which runs with the device mutex held.
Return -EBUSY if a PCI driver is already attached. Also call unlock
during PCI device removal or TSM unregister, using the same lock order.
Guest TSM unregister processes one PCI device at a time and releases
both locks before moving to the next device.

Require ARCH_HAS_CC_PLATFORM and select CONFIDENTIAL_DEVICES so
pci_tsm_unlock() clears the device's accepted state.

Expose the TSM class device's pci_mode attribute so userspace can
distinguish host link TSM from guest device-security TSM. Evidence
retrieval and acceptance are handled separately through the attestation
subsystem.

Based on the original Device Security LOCK patch from
Dan Williams <djbw@kernel.org>, adapted to the current PCI/TSM structures
and locking rules.

Signed-off-by: Aneesh Kumar K.V (Arm) <aneesh.kumar@kernel.org>

[PATCH v1 06/10] PCI/TSM: Notify TSM drivers before waiting for TSM user references
Author: Aneesh Kumar K.V (Arm) <aneesh.kumar@kernel.org>

Call the optional prepare_remove callback from tsm_unregister() before
waiting for TSM user references. Host PF0 callbacks run with
pci_tsm_rwsem held for write, followed by pci_tsm_pf0::lock. Guest
device-security callbacks take device::mutex before pci_tsm_rwsem for
write, matching PCI DMA preparation.

Later CCA patches use this callback to reject new host physical device
(PDEV) operations and guest evidence requests before unregister waits
for existing users. The guest DMA patch also uses this ordering to
reject DMA-enable requests while permitting DMA disable.

Also invoke prepare_remove() from pci_tsm_destroy() before waiting for
existing struct pci_tsm_context objects to be released. Sysfs disconnect
continues to return -EBUSY while those objects remain.

Signed-off-by: Aneesh Kumar K.V (Arm) <aneesh.kumar@kernel.org>

[PATCH v1 05/10] PCI/TSM: Add association locking helpers for evidence acceptance
Author: Aneesh Kumar K.V (Arm) <aneesh.kumar@kernel.org>

Later attestation patches let userspace finish device setup through
evidence-accept after host sysfs connect or guest sysfs lock returns.
Those commands use a saved struct pci_tsm and must prevent disconnect or
guest unlock from changing pci_dev::tsm or freeing the saved structure
while setup is in progress.

Add pci_tsm_trylock_association() to acquire pci_tsm_rwsem for write and
check that pci_dev::tsm still points to the saved structure. For a TSM
implementing devsec_ops, first take device::mutex to prevent driver
attachment during MMIO validation and device acceptance. This matches
the device-mutex-before-rwsem order used by PCI DMA preparation.

Neither lock acquisition waits. Host disconnect holds pci_tsm_rwsem for
write; guest unlock holds device::mutex and the rwsem. Both unregister
attestation, which takes ctlv::registration_lock for write to wait for
running CTLV ioctls. The evidence-accept ioctl holds that lock for read
when calling this helper, so waiting for disconnect or unlock would
deadlock. Return ERR_PTR(-EAGAIN) on contention and ERR_PTR(-ENODEV) if
the saved pointer no longer matches.

Signed-off-by: Aneesh Kumar K.V (Arm) <aneesh.kumar@kernel.org>
---
 drivers/pci/tsm.c       | 66 +++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++
 include/linux/pci-tsm.h | 17 +++++++++++
 2 files changed, 83 insertions(+)

[PATCH v1 04/10] PCI/TSM: Defer TSM vDEVICE allocation until device setup completes
Author: Aneesh Kumar K.V (Arm) <aneesh.kumar@kernel.org>

A successful connect does not necessarily complete the checks and setup
needed to assign a device to a confidential guest. Authentication and
userspace evidence acceptance may still be required.

Add pci_tsm_pf0::ready and pci_tsm_set_ready(). While ready is false,
pci_tsm_context_get() returns -EACCES, preventing TSM virtual device
(vDEVICE) allocation.

Initialize ready to true to preserve existing TSM driver behavior. A
follow-up CCA patch will clear ready during connect and set it after
evidence-accept completes the required device setup.

Signed-off-by: Aneesh Kumar K.V (Arm) <aneesh.kumar@kernel.org>

[PATCH v1 01/10] PCI/TSM: Report active IDE streams per host bridge
Author: Aneesh Kumar K.V (Arm) <aneesh.kumar@kernel.org>


The first attempt at an ABI for this failed to account for naming
collisions across host bridges:

Commit a4438f06b1db ("PCI/TSM: Report active IDE streams")

Revive this ABI with a per host bridge link that appears at first stream
creation for a given host bridge and disappears after the last stream is
removed.

For systems with many host bridge objects it allows:

    ls /sys/class/tsm/tsmN/pci*/stream*

...to find all the host bridges with active streams without first iterating
over all host bridges. Yilun notes that is handy to have this short cut [1]
and from an administrator perspective it helps with inventory for
constrained stream resources.

[PATCH v1 00/10] PCI/TSM: Support guest TSM drivers
Author: Aneesh Kumar K.V (Arm) <aneesh.kumar@kernel.org>

This series adds the PCI/TSM support needed by a TEE Security Manager
(TSM) driver in a confidential guest. It adds device lock/unlock,
helpers for recording device acceptance and parsing MMIO reports, and
DMA callbacks used during PCI driver attachment. The guest TSM driver and
attestation commands using these interfaces are in follow-up series.

This series is based on Dan Williams' "[PATCH v2 00/19] PCI/TSM: TEE I/O
infrastructure" posting [1], particularly the guest LOCK/UNLOCK, ACCEPT
and MMIO report work [2-4]. It retains device_cc_accepted() and the
confidential-device acceptance model from that posting, with changes
adapted to the current PCI/TSM structures and reference APIs. Dan's
device-core acceptance helper and per-host-bridge IDE stream reporting
patch are also included.

This series does not adopt the Device Trust changes from Dan's "Device
Evidence and Trust for PCI Security Protocol (TDISP)" [5]. That work is
being developed separately in Leon Romanovsky's "driver core: Authorize
device trust before driver binding" series, [6]. Confidential-device
acceptance is kept separate from that driver-binding trust policy.

The guest flow separates three operations:

* Lock the device through tsm/lock. This requests the TEE Device Interface
  Security Protocol (TDISP) LOCKED state, so userspace can verify evidence
  about the locked configuration. Unlock releases the device configuration
  and the TSM driver's per-device state. Neither operation is permitted
  while a PCI driver is attached.
* Request device use through the attestation evidence-accept operation.
  A follow-up guest TSM driver validates the reported MMIO mappings,
  requests TDISP RUN and marks the device accepted. This series supplies
  the device acceptance state, MMIO report helpers and PCI/TSM locking
  helpers needed for that work.
* Enable DMA during PCI driver attachment, after platform DMA configuration
  and IOMMU setup, before the PCI driver's probe function. Disable DMA
  during driver detach and setup cleanup. Hold TSM user, device and module
  references until DMA cleanup finishes calling the TSM driver.

Compared with the earlier posting, evidence retrieval and acceptance use
attestation rather than PCI/TSM evidence storage and the sysfs accept
attribute.

This series is based on the IOMMUFD/TSM vIOMMU and vDEVICE work [7]. It
uses that series' TSM references, unregister waits and struct pci_tsm_context
references. The architecture-specific CCA host and guest drivers and their
SPDM attestation operations are in separate follow-up series.

The full patchset, including attestation, Device Trust, IOMMUFD,
guest_memfd and the Arm CCA host and guest changes, is available at:

  https://git.gitlab.arm.com/linux-arm/linux-cca.git
  branch: cca/topics/7.4/cca-da-integration-v1

Notes:
* Codex assisted with implementation, review and series organization.

[PATCH v3] KVM: riscv: Allow rescheduling during G-stage unmap
Author: Can Qi <qican5708@163.com>

Removing a populated memslot or destroying a VM can require walking a
large G-stage range under mmu_lock. Allow these paths to drop the write
lock and reschedule during the walk.

Snapshot the shared root under mmu_lock and verify it before walking
again after a yield. Flush each pending batch before dropping the lock
so another invalidator cannot miss PTEs that the walker cleared.

Return the number of pages in the trailing range that still needs a
flush. The memslot and MMU notifier paths can then flush just that
range while holding mmu_lock. Detaching the root still requires a
full VM flush before freeing it, even when the last batch was empty.

Free the VM-owned split-page cache in kvm_arch_destroy_vm(), after MMU
notifier callbacks have completed. Both address-space teardown and
notifier unregister can invoke the release callback.

Codex assisted with implementation and validation. Sashiko identified
the redundant final range flush during review.

Co-developed-by: Quan Zhou <zhouquan@iscas.ac.cn>
Signed-off-by: Quan Zhou <zhouquan@iscas.ac.cn>
Signed-off-by: Can Qi <qican5708@163.com>
---
Changes in v3

- Carry the unflushed tail length back to the memslot and MMU notifier
  callers. A v2 trace showed a final request for all 262144 pages of a
  memslot when only 255 pages remained after the last batch.

Changes in v2

- Skip the final range flush when the walk has already synchronized
  every cleared PTE. Keep the full flush when detaching the root.

Testing

- Built the RV64 kernel image and modules and ran mmu_stress_test
  -c 2 -m 2 -s 1 under QEMU. The trace showed a final memslot flush
  of 154 of 262144 pages and a notifier flush of 506 of 262144
  pages. Root teardown still requested a full flush with an empty tail.

 arch/riscv/include/asm/kvm_gstage.h |  15 +++-
 arch/riscv/kvm/gstage.c             |  35 +++++++--
 arch/riscv/kvm/mmu.c                | 112 ++++++++++++++--------------
 arch/riscv/kvm/vm.c                 |   3 +
 4 files changed, 96 insertions(+), 69 deletions(-)

[PATCH v2] KVM: riscv: Allow rescheduling during G-stage unmap
Author: Can Qi <qican5708@163.com>

Removing a populated memslot or destroying a VM can require walking a
large G-stage range while holding mmu_lock. Let these callers use the
existing write-lock reschedule helper to bound the time spent holding
the lock continuously.

Snapshot the shared root under mmu_lock and stop the walk if the root
has been detached while the lock was dropped. Flush pending batches
before yielding so a concurrent invalidation cannot miss PTEs cleared
by the current walker. Finish memslot invalidation under the write
lock and synchronize the VM before releasing a detached root.
Return whether any cleared PTEs still need a flush at the end of the
walk, so callers can skip a second flush when the batches have
already been synchronized.

Free the VM-owned split-page cache in kvm_arch_destroy_vm(), after
notifier callbacks have completed. Notifier release can be invoked
by both address-space teardown and notifier unregister.

The first revision passed the QEMU KVM selftests, KASAN and lockdep
runs, and tracing of rescheduling and remote TLB request handling.
For v2, rebuild the kernel and modules and rerun the two-vCPU MMU
stress selftest. Tracing confirms the full flush still runs on root
detach even when the walker returns with no pending invalidations.

Codex assisted with implementation and validation. Sashiko identified
the pending-flush ordering concern in an earlier revision.

Co-developed-by: Quan Zhou <zhouquan@iscas.ac.cn>
Signed-off-by: Quan Zhou <zhouquan@iscas.ac.cn>
Signed-off-by: Can Qi <qican5708@163.com>
---
Changes in v2

- Return only pending TLB work from kvm_riscv_gstage_unmap_range(), so
  callers can skip the final range flush if earlier batches already
  synchronized all cleared PTEs. Keep the full flush on root detach.

[PATCH v8 15/18] iommufd: Implement ioctl to mark HWPT for preservation
Author: Samiullah Khawaja <skhawaja@google.com>


Userspace provides a token to mark the HWPT for preservation. Note that
this token is not the LUO token that is used to preserve the iommufd.
Once all the required HWPT are marked for preservation, the user can
preserve the iommufd into LUO. The iommufd will preserve the HWPTs that
are marked for preservation.

The marked HWPTs are tracked using a new XArray mark protected by a new
liveupdate mutex. This mutex will also be used during iommufd
preservation to protect against any race with the mark preserve ioctl.

The HWPT token will be used during restore to identify this HWPT. The
restoration logic is not implemented and will be added later.

Signed-off-by: YiFei Zhu <zhuyifei@google.com>
Signed-off-by: Samiullah Khawaja <skhawaja@google.com>
Reviewed-by: Pranjal Shrivastava <praan@google.com>
Reviewed-by: Lu Baolu <baolu.lu@linux.intel.com>
---

 drivers/iommu/iommufd/Makefile          |  1 +

[PATCH v8 18/18] iommufd/selftest: Add test to verify iommufd preservation
Author: Samiullah Khawaja <skhawaja@google.com>

Test iommufd preservation by setting up an iommufd with a HWPT and
preserving it across live update. The test takes the VFIO cdev path of a
device bound to the vfio-pci driver, binds it to the iommufd and attaches
it to the HWPT that is marked for preservation. It also checks that the
iommufd can't be preserved without its memfd or with hole-punched pages.

Add IOMMU_TEST_OP_LIVEUPDATE_PRESERVE_DEV to preserve the device, since
the vfio cdev preservation is not available yet.

On restore, the test verifies that the restored device can't be bound to
a new iommufd, that the iommufd can't be retrieved, as the iommufd
restore is not supported yet, and that the LUO session finish fails.

Signed-off-by: Samiullah Khawaja <skhawaja@google.com>
Signed-off-by: YiFei Zhu <zhuyifei@google.com>

[PATCH v8 16/18] iommufd: Persist iommu hardware pagetables for live update
Author: Samiullah Khawaja <skhawaja@google.com>


Register iommufd with the LUO framework and implement the preserve and
unpreserve ops to save marked HWPTs.

To make sure mappings do not change during preserved state, add a
liveupdate_immutable flag to IOAS. When an HWPT is preserved, its IOAS
is marked immutable and any map/unmap attempts will fail with -EBUSY.
This is synchronized using the domains_rwsem to prevent races with
concurrent mapping operations.

The preserve callback iterates over the marked HWPTs, verifies that the
backing memory pages are preserved, and calls iommu_preserve_domain() to
preserve the associated IOMMU domain.

Signed-off-by: YiFei Zhu <zhuyifei@google.com>
Signed-off-by: Samiullah Khawaja <skhawaja@google.com>

[PATCH v8 14/18] iommu/vt-d: Handle reattach of the restored domain
Author: Samiullah Khawaja <skhawaja@google.com>

Reattach the restored domain to the preserved device using restored
domain ID. While reattaching do not setup the context and PASID entries
as those are preserved during liveupdate.

Note that in this phase nothing clears IOMMU_SER_FLAG_INCOMING of the
restored domain or the restored state of the device, so the restored
domain cannot be replaced yet. Replacing it is left to a later phase.

[PATCH v8 08/18] iommu/vt-d: Clear unpreserved context entries during shutdown
Author: Samiullah Khawaja <skhawaja@google.com>

During normal shutdown the iommu translation is disabled. Since the root
table is preserved during live update, it needs to be cleaned up and the
context entries of the unpreserved devices and root entries for the
unpreserved context tables need to be cleared.

This is required because during kexec reboot and shutdown the devices do
not go through the release flow, so there might be stale entries in the
root table and context tables. Also note that new unpreserved context
tables for unpreserved devices might have been added after preservation,
so the root table entries for unpreserved context tables also need to be
removed.

Only the translation state of the preserved IOMMU is preserved. The next
kernel sets up its own invalidation queue and fault and page request
interrupts, so disable the invalidation queue and mask these interrupts
during shutdown.

Signed-off-by: Samiullah Khawaja <skhawaja@google.com>
Reviewed-by: Lu Baolu <baolu.lu@linux.intel.com>
---
 drivers/iommu/intel/iommu.c      |  15 ++-
 drivers/iommu/intel/iommu.h      |   5 +
 drivers/iommu/intel/liveupdate.c | 166 +++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++
 3 files changed, 184 insertions(+), 2 deletions(-)

[PATCH v8 07/18] iommu/vt-d: Implement device and iommu
Author: Samiullah Khawaja <skhawaja@google.com>

Add implementation of the device and iommu preservation in a separate
file. Also set the device and iommu preserve/unpreserve ops in the
struct iommu_ops.

Preservation is refused for devices where ATS is supported. Carrying an
active ATS configuration across kexec would require adopting the ATS
state in the PCI core and in the context entries, and handling the
disable-to-enable, enable-to-disable and STU mismatch cases for a live
device.

Note that the preserved iommu root table, context table and device pasid
table will need cleanup as devices do not go through the release flow
during kexec. So the stale entries need to be removed during shutdown.
This is done in the next commit.

Signed-off-by: Samiullah Khawaja <skhawaja@google.com>
Reviewed-by: Lu Baolu <baolu.lu@linux.intel.com>

[PATCH v8 04/18] iommupt: Implement preserve/unpreserve/restore callbacks
Author: Samiullah Khawaja <skhawaja@google.com>

Add iommupt ops for preservation, unpreservation and restoration of iommu
page tables for liveupdate. Use the existing page walker to preserve the
ioptdesc of the top_table and the lower tables.

Preserve top_level, VASZ and FEAT Sign Extended to restore the domain in
the next kernel. On restore, the domain has only the preserved features
enabled and all the other features are zeroed. This is ok since the
restored domain is made immutable and can only be freed. A kunit test is
added to verify that the IOMMU domain free can be done with trimmed
features.

[PATCH v8 00/18] iommu: Add live update state preservation
Author: Samiullah Khawaja <skhawaja@google.com>

Hi,

This patch series introduces a mechanism for IOMMU state preservation
across live update, including the Intel VT-d driver support
implementation.

Please take a look at the following LWN article to learn about KHO and
Live Update Orchestrator:

https://lwn.net/Articles/1033364/

This series is based on linux-next (next-20261120), which already has
the PCI base device preservation series and the LUO internal API to get
incoming/outgoing files.

The dependency on the VFIO cdev preservation series is removed in this
version. The patch that triggered the preservation of the iommufd state
of a vfio cdev is dropped, so this series can be applied and reviewed on
its own and merged without the VFIO series. Once the VFIO series is
ready, an extra patch glues it with the iommufd preservation. The series
with the extra patches is available at the following Github location:

https://github.com/samikhawaja/linux/tree/iommu/phase1-v8-vfio

Also note that patch 01 is taken from the VFIO cdev preservation series.
It exports the LUO file handler registration functions, which iommufd
also needs as it can be built as a module. It will be dropped from this
series once that patch is merged separately.

The kernel tree with all dependencies is uploaded to the following
Github location:

https://github.com/samikhawaja/linux/tree/iommu/phase1-v8

The series is targeting IOMMU tree.

Overall Goals:

The goal of this effort is to preserve the IOMMU domains, managed by
iommufd, attached to devices preserved through VFIO cdev. This allows
DMA mappings and IOMMU context of a device assigned to a VM to be
maintained across a kexec live update.

This is achieved by preserving IOMMU page tables using Generic Page
Table support, IOMMU root table and the relevant context entries across
live update.

The functionality in the previously sent RFC is split into two phases
and this series implements the Phase 1. Phase 1 implements the following
functionality:

  - Foundational work in IOMMU core and VT-d driver to preserve and
    restore IOMMU translation units, IOMMU domains and devices across
    liveupdate kexec.
  - The preservation is triggered by preserving vfio cdev FD and bound
    iommufd FD into a live update session.
  - An HWPT (and backing IOMMU domain) is only preserved if it contains
    only file type DMA mappings. Also the memfd behind such a mapping
    must have been preserved already.
  - During live update boot, the state of preserved Intel VT-d, IOMMU
    domain and devices is restored.
  - The restored IOMMU domains are reattached to the preserved devices
    during early boot.
  - The DMA ownership of restored devices is also claimed during live
    update boot. This means that any attempt to bind a non-vfio driver
    with them or binding a new iommufd with them will fail.

Architectural Overview:

The target architecture for IOMMU state preservation across a live
update involves coordination between the Live Update Orchestrator,
iommufd, and the IOMMU drivers.

The core design uses the Live Update Orchestrator's file descriptor
preservation mechanism to preserve iommufd file descriptors. The user
marks the iommufd HWPTs for preservation using a new ioctl added in this
series. Once done, the preservation of iommufd inside an LUO session is
triggered using LUO ioctls. During preservation, the LUO preserve
callback for an iommufd walks through the HWPTs it manages to identify
the ones that need to be preserved. Once identified, a new IOMMU core
API is used to preserve the iommu domain. The IOMMU core uses Generic
Page Table to preserve the page tables of these domains. The domains are
then marked as preserved.

When the user triggers the preservation of a VFIO cdev that is attached
to an iommufd that is preserved, the device attachment state of that
VFIO cdev is also preserved using an API exported by iommufd. IOMMUFD
fetches all the information that needs to be preserved and calls the
IOMMU core API to preserve the device state. The IOMMU core also
preserves state of IOMMU that is associated with this device.

The IOMMU core has LUO FLB registered with the iommufd LUO file handler
so the preserved iommu domain and iommu hardware unit state is available
during boot for early restore in the next kernel.

During boot the driver fetches the preserved state from the IOMMU core
and restores the state of preserved IOMMUs. Later when IOMMU core goes
through the devices and probes them, the iommu domains of preserved
devices are restored and the preserved devices are attached to them.
During attachment, the DMA ownership of these devices is also claimed.

Tested:

The iommufd_liveupdate_kexec_test selftest was used to verify the
preservation logic. Since vfio cdev preservation is not part of this
series, the test uses a new iommufd selftest op to trigger the device
preservation. So the kernel needs CONFIG_IOMMUFD_TEST. It was tested
using QEMU with virtual IOMMU (VT-d) support, in both legacy and
scalable mode, with virtio pcie device bound to the vfio-pci driver.
The next kernel was also booted with intel_iommu=off to verify that
VT-d is forced on.

Also Tested on an Intel machine with DSA device bound to vfio-pci
driver. Note that preservation is refused for devices where ATS is
supported, so the machine must be booted with pci=noats.

Following steps were followed for verification,

- Bind the test device with vfio-pci driver
- Run test on the machine by running

  ./iommufd_liveupdate_kexec_test <vfio-cdev-path>

- Trigger Kexec.
- After reboot, bind the device with vfio-pci driver and run the test
  again.

  ./iommufd_liveupdate_kexec_test <vfio-cdev-path> -s 2

- Test verifies that the restored device cannot be bound to a new
  iommufd, that the iommufd cannot be retrieved, as the iommufd restore
  is not supported yet, and that the session cannot be finished.

Future and in progress work:

- Phase 2 with IOMMUFD restore to reclaim the preserved vfio cdev and
  restore the preserved HWPTs.
- Full support for PASID preservation, i.e. non-zero PASIDs.
- Nested IOMMU preservation.
- Extend support to other IOMMU architectures (e.g., AMD-Vi).
- DMA Alloc preservation support, buddy allocator only.
  https://lore.kernel.org/all/20260708234854.4044652-1-skhawaja@google.com/
- Arm sMMUv3 preservation support
  https://github.com/pran005/linux/tree/wip-arm-smmu-lu

Roadmap:

  The doc below gives a break down of the overall work into Patch series
  needed to complete Liveupdate feature from IOMMU perspective:
  https://docs.google.com/document/d/1enDn-uPE9U77U-xHEnzn6HHGKiePSAtMIP8EDU3NO0M

High-Level Sequence Flow:

The following diagrams illustrate the high-level interactions during the
preservation phase. Note that function names in the diagram are kept
abbreviated to save horizontal space.

Preserve:

The preserve callbacks of the iommufd and vfio file handlers run when
userspace preserves the FDs into a live update session.

 Userspace (VMM) | LUO Core |    iommufd    |  IOMMU Core   | IOMMU Driver
-----------------|----------|---------------|---------------|-------------
                 |          |               |               |
MARK_HWPT        |          |               |               |
--------------------------->                |               |
                 |          | Mark HWPT for |               |
                 |          | preservation  |               |
                 |          |               |               |
PRESERVE         |          |               |               |
 iommufd_fd      |          |               |               |
----------------->          |               |               |
                 | preserve |               |               |
                 |---------->               |               |
                 |          | For each HWPT |               |
                 |          |-------------->                |
                 |          |               | domain_presrv |
                 |          |               |-------------->
                 |          |               |               | gpt(preserve)
                 |          |               |<--------------|
                 |          |<--------------|               |
                 |<---------|               |               |
                 |          |               |               |
...              |          |               |               |
                 |          |               |               |
PRESERVE,        |          |               |               |
 vfio_cdev_fd    |          |               |               |
----------------->          |               |               |
                 | preserve |               |               |
                 |---------->               |               |
                 |          |               |               |
                 |          | iommu_preserv |               |
                 |          | _device()     |               |
                 |          |-------------->                |
                 |          |               | preserve      |
                 |          |               | (iommu_hw)    |
                 |          |               |-------------->
                 |          |               |               | preserve(root)
                 |          |               |<--------------|
                 |          |               |               |
                 |          |               | preserve      |
                 |          |               | _device(dev)  |
                 |          |               |-------------->
                 |          |               |               | preserve(pasid)
                 |          |               |<--------------|
                 |          |<--------------|               |
                 |<---------|               |               |

Restore:

After a live update, the preserved state is restored during boot.

 Userspace (VMM) | LUO Core |    iommufd    |  IOMMU Core   | IOMMU Driver
-----------------|----------|---------------|---------------|-------------
                 |          |               |               |
                 |          |               |               | Restore
                 |          |               |               | Root, DIDs
                 |          |               |               |
                 |          |               |               | Register
                 |          |               | probe devices |
                 |          |               |               |
                 |          |               | restore       |
                 |          |               | domain        |
                 |          |               |-------------->
                 |          |               |               | restore
                 |          |               | reattach      |
                 |          |               | domain        |
                 |          |               |-------------->
                 |          |               |               |


Looking forward to your feedback on this.

Changelog:

v8:
  - Drop the dependency on the VFIO preservation series.
  - Add "liveupdate: Export symbols needed by modules".
  - Reserve the iommu group of a preserved device for live update with
    iommu_group_reserve_liveupdate().
  - Force VT-d on if the previous kernel preserved any IOMMU state.
  - Find the stage of the restored domain by reading the RID_PASID entry
    of the restored device.
  - Drop the no-op restored device checks around ATS/PRI in VT-d probe
    and release.
  - Keep the format specific features in the iommupt restore KUnit test.
  - Add the missing kernel-docs.
  - Update the selftest to preserve the device using a new iommufd
    selftest op instead of preserving the vfio cdev.
  - Check all devices in a group when restoring a preserved device.
  - Collect Reviewed-by tags.

v7:
  - Rebased on top of iommu/next (v7.4-rc1), PCI preservation v10 and
    VFIO preservation v6.
  - Use EXPORT_SYMBOL_GPL for all exported symbols.
  - Replace iommu_group_is_singleton() with iommu_group_device_count().
  - Check in iommu_preserve_device() that the domain is attached to the
    device, and drop the unused domain argument of
    iommu_unpreserve_device().
  - Drop iommu_for_each_dev(). The VT-d driver now walks the PCI devices
    and the ACPI namespace device scope of the DMAR unit to clear the
    context entries of unpreserved devices during shutdown.
  - Disable the invalidation queue and mask the fault and page request
    interrupts of preserved IOMMUs during shutdown.
  - Rename iommu_get_preserved_data(), iommu_for_each_preserved_device()
    and iommu_init_device_preserved_data() to iommu_get_restored_state(),
    iommu_for_each_restored_device() and
    iommu_init_device_restored_data().
  - Panic if the hardware translation state does not match.
  - WARN if the restored domain ID does not match.
  - Track preserved devices using idev->liveupdate_preserved.
  - Collect Reviewed-by tags.

v6:
  - Add size, features and reserved fields to the KHO ABI to manage
    compatibility.
  - Use KHO block for preservation of KHO data structures.
  - Drop "memfd: export memfd_get_seals()" and the memfd seal
    requirement as it is not reliable because memfd can still have holes
    punched after sealing.
  - Split the disable_dmar_iommu()/free_dmar_iommu() merge into a separate
    patch.
  - Move the PASID table preservation patch before the domain reattach
    patch.
  - Move iommu_phys from the device attachment into iommu_device_ser.
  - Rename vasz to max_vasz_lg2, and IOMMU_INVALID/IOMMU_INTEL to
    IOMMU_SER_TYPE_INVALID/IOMMU_SER_TYPE_INTEL.
  - Check the FLB version in retrieve before restoring the serialized
    objects.
  - Use _ser suffix consistently for serialized objects.
  - Rework iommu_for_each_dev() to remove redundant arguments.
  - Sanity check scalable mode changes across kexec.
  - Use context_tables_bitmap as the source of truth during restore.
  - Reuse reserve_domain_id() to reclaim the preserved domain IDs.
  - Drop the IOMMU restored flag and panic if DRHD unit restore fails.
  - Rename intel_iommu_restore_device() to
    intel_iommu_attach_restored_device(), and the domain reattach/detach
    helpers to domain_attach_restored_iommu()/domain_detach_restored_iommu().
  - Drop the cache tag and IOPF setup for the restored domain.
  - Rename nr_preserved_domains, nr_hwpts and nr_liveupdate_preserved to
    num_preserved_domains, num_hwpts and num_liveupdate_preserved.
  - Update the selftest to use an unsealed memfd and verify that a
    hole-punched mapping is rejected.

v5:
  - Rebased on top of PCI preservation v9 and VFIO preservation v5.
  - Do not preserve devices that share iommu groups.
  - Save the DMA owner (iommufd) token in the serialized device state.
  - Use READ_ONCE/WRITE_ONCE for dev->iommu->device_ser.
  - Refuse preservation of devices where ATS is supported. Also drop PRE
    disable as it depends on ATS.
  - Drop the fallback stubs for the preserve/unpreserve ops.
  - Clear the present bit and flush before clearing the context entry.
  - Add comments on global invalidation and why the IOMMU lock is not
    needed during shutdown.
  - Treat -ENODATA, -ENOENT and -EOPNOTSUPP as absent FLB during
    retrieve and fail only on other errors.
  - Reclaim DID by first checking if the DID is already reclaimed by
    another preserved device.
  - Handle release device by doing teardown and reattach of restored
    domain to restored devices.
  - Validate the domain physical address, the restored domain identity
    and the IOMMU identity before reattaching.

  - Rename setup_cdev to open_cdev.
  - Define constants (tokens) as u64 to avoid ABI warnings.
