[PATCH v2] vhost/net: ignore extra words in VHOST_SET_FEATURES_ARRAY
Author: Hengyu Liang <hengyul@cs.unc.edu>

Commit 333c515d1896 ("vhost-net: allow configuring extended features")
added VHOST_SET_FEATURES_ARRAY. The ioctl reads the number of feature
words from user space, copies the words it supports, and then reads
every remaining word, one at a time, to check that it is zero.

That loop has no upper bound and no scheduling point. A caller can pass
a count of U64_MAX and a pointer into a large zero-filled mapping. The
ioctl then stays in the kernel for as long as the mapping is readable,
and a fatal signal is not handled until it ends. On a v6.18 debug kernel
booted with preempt=none, two such calls from a non-root user, each on a
4 GiB mapping, froze a 2-CPU VM for 56 s with soft lockup reports.

The check is not needed: the extra words can only hold features that
this kernel does not know about, and they are never stored. Drop the
loop and ignore them. A nonzero extra word is now ignored instead of
failing with EOPNOTSUPP. Unsupported bits in the words the kernel knows
still fail with EOPNOTSUPP.

Fixes: 333c515d1896 ("vhost-net: allow configuring extended features")
Cc: stable@vger.kernel.org # v6.17+
Suggested-by: Michael S. Tsirkin <mst@redhat.com>
Signed-off-by: Hengyu Liang <hengyul@cs.unc.edu>
---
Changes in v2:
- Drop the loop instead of rejecting counts above one page (Michael).
- v1: https://lore.kernel.org/r/20261122120919.1718445-1-hengyul@cs.unc.edu

I found this while comparing the behavior of different kernel versions.
I used an LLM to analyze the problem and to draft the patch and this
message, and I analyzed the result myself.

Tested on the vhost tree at 44293796526f with KASAN and lockdep. With a
count of U64_MAX on a 1 GiB zero-filled mapping, the ioctl now returns
at once (before: EFAULT after 27.4 s), and a SIGKILL sent during the
call no longer waits for the walk. Counts of 0, 2, 3, 512 and 514 with
zero words succeed as before. Unsupported bits in the first two words
still give EOPNOTSUPP. A nonzero third word now succeeds instead of
giving EOPNOTSUPP. VHOST_GET_FEATURES_ARRAY is unchanged.

===== Patch Commit Messages ====

[PATCH] KVM: arm64: nv: Allow the L1 VNCR page in cacheable PFNMAP memory
Author: Jia He <justin.he@arm.com>

kvm_translate_vncr() refuses any PFN that isn't memory and injects an
SEA into the guest hypervisor. That is stricter than what the stage-2
fault path allows (kvm_s2_fault_pin_pfn()): a VM_PFNMAP or VM_MIXEDMAP
VMA whose host stage-1 attributes are cacheable is mapped cacheable at
stage-2 when FWB and DIC are present, and the guest can use such memory
as RAM (e.g. device memory that it onlines as a NUMA node). If the guest
then picks a page of that memory as the VNCR page of one of its own
guests, it takes an SEA as soon as it enters that guest.

Relax the check to match the stage-2 fault path, addressing the FIXME
added by commit 4bd7dbe0b224 ("KVM: arm64: nv: Inject SEA if guest VNCR
isn't normal memory"): look up the VMA, and allow the PFN if the VMA is
a cacheable VM_PFNMAP or VM_MIXEDMAP one and the system supports
cacheable PFNMAP at stage-2. No linear mapping is required, as VNCR
accesses use the EL2 fixmap and require no cache maintenance.
mmu_invalidate_seq is sampled before the PFN is faulted in, so a VMA
change after the lookup is caught by the mmu_invalidate_retry() check
that follows, as for the PFN itself.

Tested on a host with FEAT_FGT, FWB and DIC, with an L1 whose whole RAM
is a cacheable PFNMAP (host RAM withheld with mem= and handed to the VMM
by a test driver with remap_pfn_range()) starting an L2 guest
hypervisor. Without the patch, L2 repeatedly takes an SEA at the same PC
and fails to boot. With the patch, L2 boots successfully.

Signed-off-by: Jia He <justin.he@arm.com>
---

[PATCH v2 0/2] KVM: arm64: nv: Fix stale and duplicate guest S2
Author: Colton Lewis <coltonlewis@google.com>

When an L1 guest issues TLBI IPAS2E1 with a TTL hint smaller than the
actual mapping, KVM clears the entire block from the shadow stage-2
page table while leaving the stale tracking entry in guest_s2_mappings.
Patch 1 drops undersized TTL hints in compute_tlb_inval_range() and
falls through to the unhinted path so the unmap and tracking removal
cover the same range. This follows Marc's suggestion [2] on Dan
Carpenter's sub-page TTL patch [1] (which Patch 1 overlaps with and
subsumes), fixing WARN_ON(size & ~PAGE_MASK) on 16kB/64kB hosts as well.

Patch 2 avoids inserting identical duplicate entries in
kvm_record_guest_s2_mapping().

Tool-generated content:
- Initial bug discovery: automated LLM-assisted audit of the arm64
  nested stage-2 tracking code.
- Patch and text drafting: interactive Gemini session (patches, commit
  messages, and cover letter).

Changes since v1:
- Drop the kvm_remove_guest_s2_mappings() change; discard undersized TTL
  hints in compute_tlb_inval_range() and fall through to the unhinted
  path (Marc Zyngier).
- Split duplicate suppression in kvm_record_guest_s2_mapping() into a
  separate patch, call kfree() under guard(spinlock), and drop the Fixes
  tag (Wei-Lin Chang).
- Rewrite the commit messages and cover letter.

Link to v1: https://lore.kernel.org/r/20261119185105.626343-1-coltonlewis@google.com
[1] https://lore.kernel.org/r/20261123-inval_range-v1-1-815e15797089@pereq.eu
[2] https://lore.kernel.org/r/86h5h7zn5a.wl-maz@kernel.org

    compute_tlb_inval_range()

[PATCH v2 2/2] KVM: arm64: nv: Avoid duplicate guest stage-2 mapping entries
Author: Colton Lewis <coltonlewis@google.com>

kvm_record_guest_s2_mapping() unconditionally inserts a new entry into
both guest_s2_mappings interval trees. Parallel stage-2 faults on the
same IPA or retries after -EAGAIN can therefore insert identical
entries for the same nested and canonical IPA range.

Check mmu->guest_s2_mappings under guest_s2_tracking_lock before
inserting, and free the new entry if an identical one is already
present.

Signed-off-by: Colton Lewis <coltonlewis@google.com>
---
 arch/arm64/kvm/nested.c | 29 +++++++++++++++++++++++++++++
 1 file changed, 29 insertions(+)

[PATCH v2 1/2] KVM: arm64: nv: Discard undersized TTL hints in compute_tlb_inval_range()
Author: Colton Lewis <coltonlewis@google.com>

When an L1 guest issues TLBI IPAS2E1 with a non-zero TTL hint,
compute_tlb_inval_range() takes it at face value. If the hinted size is
smaller than the actual mapping (for example, a 4kB L3 hint on a 2MB
block), kvm_pgtable_stage2_unmap() clears the entire block while
kvm_remove_guest_s2_mappings() leaves its stale tracking entry behind in
guest_s2_mappings. On 16kB or 64kB hosts, a 4kB hint also triggers
WARN_ON(size & ~PAGE_MASK) in kvm_stage2_unmap_range().

A TTL hint smaller than PAGE_SIZE or the mapped entry is wrong. Check
it against PAGE_SIZE, the shadow stage-2 leaf size, and the guest block
size encoded in KVM_NV_GUEST_MAP_SZ. If it is too small, ignore the hint
and fall through to the unhinted invalidation path.

When falling back to the maximum mapping extent with stage-2 disabled,
size it from PAGE_SIZE rather than VTCR_EL2.TG0 so 1:1 shadow blocks are
still covered.

Fixes: d1de1576dc21 ("KVM: arm64: nv: Handle FEAT_TTL hinted TLB operations")
Signed-off-by: Colton Lewis <coltonlewis@google.com>
---

[PATCH 1/3] KVM: arm64: Add enum support to idreg sanitisation
Author: Marc Zyngier <maz@kernel.org>

While a good deal of ID register fields are simple booleans, simply
indicating whether a feature is supported, a number of them are more
structured and indicate a level of support.

Reuse the existing framework in a nested way to expose which level
applies for the current configuration. This relies on the levels
being ordered from the most to the least capable, and the fallback
to have a NULL callback.

Yes, this is pretty obscure, but the following patches will make
this clearer (at least one hopes...).

Signed-off-by: Marc Zyngier <maz@kernel.org>

[PATCH] KVM: arm64: nv: Upgrade sub-page TTL hint to maximum size
Author: Marc Zyngier <maz@kernel.org>

Should a guest be slightly foolish and submit a S2 TLBI with a TTL hint
that describe a base granule size smaller than the host's page size,
we take the resulting size at face value and feed it to the S2 unmapping
code. Which legitimately screams about something not being page-aligned.

The architecture allows us to drop the TLBI on the floor, as we don't
support a S2 granule size smaller than the host's page size. But it turns
out that doing so is proving pretty hard (we call compute_tlb_inval_range
from a number of places, none of which is amenable to an early out).

Instead, detect the annoying case, and pretend that we don't have a TTL
hint at all, leaving the size upgrade mechanism to take place and use
the largest possible size for the guest's S2 granule size.

This will still allow nonsensical stuff to get through, such as a 4kB
level 2 (aka 2MB) from a guest with a 64kB S2 base granule size running
on a 16kB page size host, but that's not worse than doing nothing, and
the host code won't get shouty.

Reported-by: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>
Link: https://lore.kernel.org/r/20261123-inval_range-v1-1-815e15797089@pereq.eu
Fixes: d1de1576dc21 ("KVM: arm64: nv: Handle FEAT_TTL hinted TLB operations")
Signed-off-by: Marc Zyngier <maz@kernel.org>
Cc: stable@vger.kernel.org
---

Notes:
    Reposting this as a first class patch, as I'd like to plug that one
    as early as possible (and Dan has plenty of other patches in flight...)

[PATCH 2/3] KVM: arm64: nv: Don't request or attempt L1 VNCR mapping without vncr_tlb
Author: Jia He <justin.he@arm.com>

vcpu->arch.vncr_tlb is only allocated when ID_AA64MMFR4_EL1.NV_frac
advertises FEAT_NV2. When NV_frac is cleared to 0 (or E2H0 is selected),
vncr_tlb is NULL for the lifetime of the vCPU.

Don't set KVM_REQ_MAP_L1_VNCR_EL2 in kvm_vcpu_load_hw_mmu() when
vncr_tlb is NULL. The only other place that sets this request is
kvm_translate_vncr(), which only runs on a FEAT_NV2 VNCR abort. Warn and
return early in kvm_map_l1_vncr() if vt is NULL before disabling
preemption and taking kvm->mmu_lock.

Signed-off-by: Jia He <justin.he@arm.com>

[PATCH 1/3] KVM: arm64: Don't load hardware vCPU state before first run
Author: Jia He <justin.he@arm.com>

KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY calls vcpu_load() as soon as KVM_ARM_VCPU_INIT has
completed. On arm64, however, kvm->arch.sysreg_masks, the trap
configuration, vncr_tlb and nested vGIC state are only set up on the
first KVM_RUN in kvm_arch_vcpu_run_pid_change().

Running the full vcpu_load() path before then reads unsanitised guest
sysregs and touches uninitialised nested state: get_s2_mmu_nested()
populates nested_mmus[] from raw VTTBR_EL2/VTCR_EL2/HCR_EL2 values, FGT
and HCRX_EL2 are written before kvm_calculate_traps() has run, and
kvm_vgic_load() can enter vgic_v3_load_nested() early. In particular, if
userspace creates an EL2 vCPU with ID_AA64MMFR4_EL1.NV_frac cleared to 0
(so vncr_tlb is never allocated), sets HCR_EL2.NV=1 and an EL1 PSTATE,
and calls KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY, kvm_vcpu_load_hw_mmu() queues
KVM_REQ_MAP_L1_VNCR_EL2. The next KVM_RUN then dereferences a NULL
vncr_tlb in kvm_map_l1_vncr() with preemption disabled and kvm->mmu_lock
held for read:

  [   73.636014] Unable to handle kernel NULL pointer dereference at virtual address 0000000000000078
  [   73.801972] pc : kvm_map_l1_vncr+0x48/0x2d0
  [   73.884820] Call trace:
  [   73.887253]  kvm_map_l1_vncr+0x48/0x2d0 (P)
  [   73.891422]  check_nested_vcpu_requests+0xb8/0x168
  [   73.896200]  check_vcpu_requests+0x2a0/0x428
  [   73.900458]  kvm_arch_vcpu_ioctl_run+0x2d8/0x6d8
  [   73.905062]  kvm_vcpu_ioctl+0x388/0xaf0

KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY still needs generic vcpu_load() to set
kvm_running_vcpu for dirty-ring tracking in mark_page_dirty_in_slot(),
but kvm_arch_vcpu_pre_fault_memory() only walks and populates
&kvm->arch.mmu and does not use any hardware vCPU state. Skip the arch
vcpu_load()/vcpu_put() callbacks until vcpu_has_run_once() is true. In
KVM_RUN, vcpu->pid is set under vcpu->mutex right after
kvm_arch_vcpu_run_pid_change() succeeds and before
kvm_arch_vcpu_ioctl_run() calls vcpu_load().

Since kvm_running_vcpu can now be set without VHE state on the CPU, use
host_ctxt->__hyp_running_vcpu in VHE enter_vmid_context() (matching
nVHE) and check vcpu_has_run_once() in vgic_maintenance_handler().

Fixes: 34cfa9b3af17 ("KVM: arm64: Implement KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY")
Signed-off-by: Jia He <justin.he@arm.com>

[PATCH 0/3] KVM: arm64: Fix pre-first-run KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY vcpu_load() issues
Author: Jia He <justin.he@arm.com>

KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY calls vcpu_load() as soon as KVM_ARM_VCPU_INIT has
completed. On arm64, VM and vCPU state such as kvm->arch.sysreg_masks,
trap controls, vncr_tlb and nested vGIC setup is only initialised on the
first KVM_RUN in kvm_arch_vcpu_run_pid_change().

Running the full arch vcpu_load() path before then reads unsanitised
guest sysregs and touches uninitialised nested state. In particular, if
a VMM creates an EL2 vCPU with ID_AA64MMFR4_EL1.NV_frac cleared to 0,
sets HCR_EL2.NV=1 and an EL1 PSTATE, and calls KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY,
kvm_vcpu_load_hw_mmu() queues KVM_REQ_MAP_L1_VNCR_EL2 without a
vncr_tlb. The next KVM_RUN then dereferences NULL in kvm_map_l1_vncr()
with preemption disabled and kvm->mmu_lock held for read.

Patch 1 skips the arch vcpu_load()/vcpu_put() callbacks until
vcpu_has_run_once() is true, and adjusts VHE enter_vmid_context() and
vgic_maintenance_handler() accordingly. Generic vcpu_load() still sets
kvm_running_vcpu for dirty-ring tracking, and pre-faulting only populates
&kvm->arch.mmu.

Patch 2 checks that vncr_tlb is allocated before setting
KVM_REQ_MAP_L1_VNCR_EL2 and adds a WARN_ON_ONCE(!vt) check in
kvm_map_l1_vncr().

Patch 3 extends nv_pre_fault_memory_test to cover pre-first-run
pre-faulting. The cases that do not need EL2 run on all hosts. On an
unfixed kernel, the staged nested context case triggers the "Separate
virtual IRQ/FIQ settings not supported" WARN while the test still
passes, so the kernel log must be checked as well.

  KVM: arm64: Don't load hardware vCPU state before first run
  KVM: arm64: nv: Don't request or attempt L1 VNCR mapping without
    vncr_tlb
  KVM: selftests: arm64: Test KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY before first KVM_RUN

[PATCH v3 1/3] KVM: Stop KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY when the vCPU's dirty ring is full
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

Pre-faulting can map a dirty-logged page writable, which pushes it to
the vCPU's dirty ring. KVM_RUN exits with KVM_EXIT_DIRTY_RING_FULL
before the ring overflows, but the pre-fault loop never checks it, so
one call can push more entries than the ring has free, which trips the
WARN in kvm_dirty_ring_push() and overwrites entries userspace hasn't
harvested. On x86:

  WARNING: arch/x86/kvm/../../../virt/kvm/dirty_ring.c:225 at kvm_dirty_ring_push+0x7a/0xd0
  Call Trace:
   make_spte+0x2cc/0x450
   kvm_tdp_mmu_map+0x5e1/0x7f0
   kvm_tdp_page_fault+0x12c/0x1b0
   kvm_mmu_do_page_fault+0x181/0x250
   kvm_arch_vcpu_pre_fault_memory+0x136/0x1d0
   kvm_vcpu_pre_fault_memory+0xcf/0x180
   kvm_vcpu_ioctl+0x455/0x770

Stop the loop once the ring is soft full, with the check KVM_RUN makes
before entering the guest. Return the pages already processed, or
-ENOSPC when there aren't any, since a retry can't succeed until
userspace harvests and resets the ring. s390 has the ioctl but no ring,
so give kvm_dirty_ring_check_request() a stub.

The check also sets run->exit_reason to KVM_EXIT_DIRTY_RING_FULL. On
arm64, KVM_RUN completes a pending MMIO exit only if exit_reason is
still KVM_EXIT_MMIO, and on riscv KVM_RUN does the same for its MMIO,
SBI and CSR exits. If a pre-fault stops on a full ring while such an
exit is pending, the next KVM_RUN doesn't complete it and the guest
repeats the instruction that caused the exit. Userspace can avoid that
by completing the exit with KVM_RUN before pre-faulting.

Fixes: bc1a5cd002116 ("KVM: Add KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY vcpu ioctl to pre-populate guest memory")
Cc: stable@vger.kernel.org
Signed-off-by: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>
---
 Documentation/virt/kvm/api.rst | 9 +++++++--
 include/linux/kvm_dirty_ring.h | 5 +++++
 virt/kvm/kvm_main.c            | 5 +++++
 3 files changed, 17 insertions(+), 2 deletions(-)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

[PATCH v3 3/3] KVM: selftests: Test KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY against a full dirty ring
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

Pre-fault more populated, dirty-logged pages than the vCPU's dirty ring
holds, and check that each call stops before the ring overflows, that a
retry returns ENOSPC without progress, and that each page is reported
exactly once. A read pre-fault can map a page read-only, or
access-tracked on x86 without EPT A/D bits, so skip the case if
pre-faulting doesn't dirty any page.

Acked-by: Lorenzo Stoakes (ARM) <ljs@kernel.org>
Signed-off-by: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>
---
 .../selftests/kvm/pre_fault_memory_test.c     | 102 ++++++++++++++++++
 1 file changed, 102 insertions(+)

[PATCH v3 2/3] KVM: Warn userspace against ioctls while an exit is pending
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY sets run->exit_reason to KVM_EXIT_DIRTY_RING_FULL
when it stops on a full dirty ring. If an MMIO exit is pending at that
point, the next KVM_RUN on arm64 and riscv doesn't complete it. Rather
than document that case alone, which could suggest that other ioctls
are safe, warn against invoking any ioctl other than KVM_RUN before a
pending exit is completed.

Suggested-by: Sean Christopherson <seanjc@google.com>
Signed-off-by: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

 Documentation/virt/kvm/api.rst | 11 +++++++----
 1 file changed, 7 insertions(+), 4 deletions(-)

[PATCH v3 0/3] KVM: Bound KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY by the vCPU's dirty ring
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

Hi folks,

Changes since v2 [1]:
  - Added patch 2, which warns in api.rst against calling ioctls other
    than KVM_RUN while an exit is pending. (Sean)
  - Mentioned riscv as well as arm64 in patch 1's exit_reason
    paragraph. (Lorenzo)
  - Collected Lorenzo's Acked-by on patch 3. (thanks!)

KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY never checks the vCPU's dirty ring, so one call
over a large enough range of populated, dirty-logged pages overflows
it. The first patch stops the loop at the soft limit, as KVM_RUN does.
The second warns userspace against calling ioctls other than
KVM_RUN while an exit is pending. The third adds the case to
pre_fault_memory_test.

Pre-faulting isn't the only ioctl that can push past the ring's
reserve. On x86, KVM_SET_MSRS does too when it writes the wall clock or
the Hyper-V hypercall page. A guest can also push past the reserve
within one exit, for example through the arm64 ITS. What those paths
should do when the ring fills is an open question for the dirty ring
API, which I don't think belongs in this series.

I didn't include the kvm_dirty_ring_check_request() rewrite that Sean
sketched on v1, since I think it can lose a request. It tests the
request first and clears it afterwards. If the vCPU is scheduled out in
between, loongarch sets the PV preempted flag in guest memory and x86
updates a Xen guest's runstate area, either of which pushes an entry.
When that push makes the ring soft full, the request it makes is then
cleared, and the vCPU runs with the ring soft full until its next push.
Clearing first and re-setting, as the code does now, doesn't have that
window.

Based on Linux 7.4-rc2 (7475ea8b58616; also applies to kvm-x86/next
cbe54fc3462b3 with git am -3).

Cheers,
/fuad

[1] https://lore.kernel.org/all/20261117074428.7544-1-fuad.tabba@linux.dev/

Fuad Tabba (3):
  KVM: Stop KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY when the vCPU's dirty ring is full
  KVM: Warn userspace against ioctls while an exit is pending
  KVM: selftests: Test KVM_PRE_FAULT_MEMORY against a full dirty ring

[PATCH v4 11/11] KVM: arm64: CCA: Add Realm stage-2 MMU operations
Author: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>


Register the Realm stage-2 MMU operations now that the RMM mapping
and unmapping helpers are available. Route unmapping through the
RMM, and use the Realm-specific fault handler for Realm memory faults.

The RMM handles TLB invalidation when stage-2 mappings change, so
the host has no separate TLB flush to perform. Realm stage-2 does
not support access flags, including for shared IPAs.

Realm teardown destroys non-root RTTs through RMI, so the Realm
stage-2 destruction callback frees only the root RTT pages.

Signed-off-by: Suzuki K Poulose <suzuki.poulose@arm.com>
Signed-off-by: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

---

- Register realm_mem_abort() as the Realm stage-2 fault handler.

Changes since v2:
- Move common stage-2 destruction dispatch to a separate patch;
  retain the Realm-specific destruction callback here.

[PATCH v4 10/11] KVM: arm64: CCA: Support runtime faulting of memory
Author: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

At runtime, if the realm guest accesses an IPA without a stage-2
mapping, then KVM fixes up the stage-2 mapping for the backing memory.

For memory in the lower (protected) region of IPA, verify that the
corresponding guest_memfd page is private before delegating to the RMM.
The RMM then maps the delegated page into the Realm.

For memory in the upper (shared) IPA region, map the backing page as
non-secure memory. Check that a guest_memfd page is shared when the
fault uses guest_memfd; ordinary userspace-backed memslots can only be
mapped at unprotected IPAs and follow the normal user memory fault path.

If the fault IPA and guest_memfd attributes disagree then exit to
userspace with KVM_EXIT_MEMORY_FAULT so the VMM can perform the required
conversion. If a shared IPA has no memslot, KVM follows the existing
MMIO or abort-injection path. The latter can result in injecting a SEA
to the guest.

Realm faults may need to create RTTs, including when updating a
mapping after a permission fault. Take the write side of mmu_lock
and top up the MMU cache for these faults. Do not pre-fault Realm
memory.

The lock helpers are in a header compiled by nVHE too. Make
kvm_vm_hyp_is_distrusting() available there for the lock choice.

Co-developed-by: Suzuki K Poulose <suzuki.poulose@arm.com>
Signed-off-by: Suzuki K Poulose <suzuki.poulose@arm.com>
Signed-off-by: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

---
Changes since v3:
- Split Realm faults into dedicated guest_memfd and userspace
  mapping paths, leaving the native fault paths unchanged.
- Unmap earlier shared mappings if a later RMI map call fails.
- Clarify that runtime RMI data mapping does not guarantee zeroed
  contents.

Changes since v2:
- Route all guest_memfd faults through gmem_abort(). Bound each
  protected RMI map call to its encoded range, create the required
  RTT levels, and tighten retry and error handling.

Changes since v1:
- Use the write side of mmu_lock and top up the MMU cache for Realm
  faults that may create RTTs. Skip unnecessary cache flushing,
  disable Realm pre-faulting, and tighten memory-fault and mapping
  retry handling.
---

[PATCH v4 08/11] KVM: Allow architectures to reject memslot changes before invalidation
Author: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>


The next patch needs to reject changes to a realm guest_memfd memslot
before invalidation. KVM currently invalidates the slot and calls
kvm_arch_flush_shadow_memslot() before kvm_arch_prepare_memory_region()
can reject the request. For a realm, invalidation unmaps stage-2
mappings; restoring the old memslot on failure does not restore those
mappings. Thus a rejected deletion or move would still have side effects.

Add kvm_arch_validate_memory_region() and invoke it before invalidating
the slot, with slots_lock and slots_arch_lock held. The hook performs
validation only, allowing architectures to reject a request without
changing the memslot or its mappings. Provide a weak no-op implementation
for architectures that do not need early validation.

Move arm64's existing pVM checks into the new hook as part of early
validation. pVM invalidation uses no_stage2_unmap_range(), so the realm
case motivates this hook. Keep resource preparation after invalidation,
since invalidation drops slots_arch_lock and allows concurrent updates
to architecture-specific memslot metadata.

Signed-off-by: Suzuki K Poulose <suzuki.poulose@arm.com>
Signed-off-by: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

---
Changes since v3:
- Clarify that Realm stage-2 invalidation motivates the early
  validation hook; pVM invalidation does not unmap stage-2.
---

 arch/arm64/kvm/pkvm.c    |  2 +-
 include/linux/kvm_host.h |  4 ++++

 4 files changed, 47 insertions(+), 14 deletions(-)

[PATCH v4 09/11] KVM: arm64: CCA: Prevent memslot modifications after Realm creation
Author: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>


Once the Realm is created, prevent changes to guest_memfd backed
memslots, which can provide private memory. Validate these changes
before KVM invalidates the old memslot, so a rejected deletion leaves
its stage-2 mappings intact. Realm stage-2 faults will also validate
the private memory backing in a later commit.

Require Realm memslots to lie in the lower half of the IPA space, as the
upper, shared half aliases the lower, protected half.

Define kvm_arch_has_private_mem() so generic KVM recognizes Realm VMs
as supporting private memory. Defining the macro also makes
gmem_in_place_conversion true for every arm64 VM, so guest_memfd
memslots are marked gmem-only even for non-Realm VMs. A well-formed
KVM_SET_MEMORY_ATTRIBUTES2 request on a non-Realm guest_memfd now
reaches the VM check and returns -EINVAL instead of -ENOTTY.

Additionally, similar to pKVM, do not support dirty logging or
read-only memslots.

Signed-off-by: Suzuki K Poulose <suzuki.poulose@arm.com>
Signed-off-by: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

---
Changes since v3:
- Explain the arm64-wide in-place conversion effect and keep the
  general IPA limit comment.

Changes since v1:
- Preserve private mappings if KVM invalidates a Realm guest_memfd
  slot before the prepare hook rejects its deletion.

[PATCH v4 06/11] KVM: arm64: CCA: Destroy RTTs
Author: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

The RMM owns the stage 2 page tables for a realm, and KVM must request
that the RMM creates/destroys entries as necessary. The physical pages
to store the page tables are delegated to the realm as required, and can
be undelegated when no longer used.

Creating new RTTs is the easy part, destroying them is a little more
tricky. The result of realm_rtt_destroy() can be used to effectively
walk the tree and destroy the entries (undelegating pages that were
given to the realm).

Tear down a created Realm whether it is still new or has been activated.
Unmap the Realm IPA space once during stage-2 teardown. Defer
destruction of non-root RTTs until final Realm destruction, after
REALM_TERMINATE and before destroying the RD; root RTTs remain until the
RD has gone away.

Use the same minimum of two stage-2 levels as VTCR when deriving
the Realm's starting RTT level. Without this, narrow IPAs on
64K-page hosts can give the RMM a different starting level from
KVM's own page tables.

Signed-off-by: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

---
Changes since v3:
- Match VTCR's minimum of two stage-2 levels for Realm RTTs.
- Use explicit config_lock cleanup in Realm destruction.

Changes since v2:
- Use the actual stage-2 start level and correct the RTT skip
  granularity. Free SRO state on final teardown errors.

Changes since v1:
- Tear down created Realms in both NEW and ACTIVE states. Fix the
  RTT start level, page accounting on failed undelegation, and
  handling of RTT granules returned by RMI.
---

 arch/arm64/kvm/mmu.c             |  17 ++-
 arch/arm64/kvm/rmi.c             | 178 +++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++
 3 files changed, 197 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-)

[PATCH v4 01/11] KVM: arm64: guest_memfd: Check mmap support when preparing memslot
Author: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

Arm64 requires mmap-capable guest_memfd backing because the memslot HVA
may still be used to access shared memory even when guest faults use
guest_memfd.

The existing check rejects guest_memfd without mmap support based on the
memslot's gmem-only flag. That stops working when arm64 defines
kvm_arch_has_private_mem() for realms: in-place conversion then marks all
guest_memfd memslots gmem-only, including those backed by files without
GUEST_MEMFD_FLAG_MMAP. Such files would pass the existing check.

Check the backing file's mmap capability directly to keep requiring it
for every arm64 VM. Expose a helper to reuse kvm_gmem_supports_mmap().

Signed-off-by: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

---

- Explain why in-place conversion makes checking the memslot's
  gmem-only flag insufficient for arm64.

Changes since v2:
- Require mmap-capable guest_memfd backing for all arm64 VMs, as
  shared-memory access can use the memslot HVA.

Changes since v1:
- New patch in v2. Check the guest_memfd backing file rather than the
  memslot flag when deciding whether arm64 can map the backing memory.

[PATCH v4 00/11] KVM: arm64: CCA: Prepare Realm memory management
Author: Steven Price <steven.price@arm.com>

This series adds the memory management plumbing for CCA, building on the
basic RMM support [1]. Thanks to everyone who reviewed and tested the
earlier versions.

The patches introduce internal Realm VM state, prepare Realm stage-2
setup and teardown, and provide RTT mapping and invalidation helpers.
Realm memslots are restricted to the lower half of the IPA space because
the protected and shared halves alias each other. The final patch
connects the helpers to KVM's stage-2 MMU operations.

This is the memory management portion of the larger Realm host series.
Realm descriptor creation, REC entry and exit, activation, and the new
uAPI follow in the integration stack. This series alone does not enable
Realm creation.

The integration branch [2] includes Realm creation, REC entry and exit,
and the remaining host support on top of this series. The MM branch [3]
contains just this series.

Changes since v3:
- Keep pKVM-specific protected VM handling in the nVHE path and move
  stage-2 destruction dispatch before basic Realm infrastructure.
- Derive the Realm RTT start level from VTCR, matching KVM's minimum
  two-level stage-2 layout for narrow IPAs on 64K-page hosts.
- Split Realm guest_memfd and userspace-backed faults into dedicated
  paths, leaving the native fault handlers unchanged.
- Unmap earlier shared mappings when a later RMI map call fails.
- Clarify the early memslot validation rationale and the arm64-wide
  effect of in-place guest_memfd conversion.

Changes since v2:
- Move RMM mode reservation and RMI availability and feature checks to
  the integration stack.
- Require mmap-capable guest_memfd backing for all arm64 VMs and route
  all guest_memfd faults through gmem_abort().
- Store the Realm Descriptor physical address from initial Realm setup,
  and improve SRO cleanup and RTT teardown error handling.
- Use a local MMU cache for RTT invalidation, handle failed splits, and
  validate returned RTT levels.
- Bound protected RMI mapping calls to the encoded range and tighten
  RTT creation, retry, and error handling.
- Split common stage-2 destruction dispatch into its own patch before
  registering the Realm stage-2 MMU operations.

Changes since v1:
- Add a guest_memfd prerequisite patch that checks whether the backing
  file supports mmap when preparing an arm64 memslot.
- Combine the RMI availability and feature checks into one patch, and
  move Realm vCPU feature restrictions to the integration stack.
- Correct Realm IPA sizing and RTT teardown, including teardown before
  activation, RTT accounting, and handling of missing or folded RTTs.
- Rework RTT mapping to respect IPA and physical alignment, handle
  partial RMI progress, and retry interrupted operations.
- Preserve private mappings when a Realm guest_memfd memslot deletion is
  rejected, and tighten Realm fault handling and stage-2 destruction.

[PATCH 0/2] KVM: arm64: Fix stage-2 THP mapping on hosts with 2-level page tables
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

Hi folks,

A fix for how KVM maps a THP at stage-2 on a host with 2-level page
tables (64K pages with a 42-bit VA, or 16K pages with a 36-bit VA).
There, KVM also maps the host memory that follows a THP into the guest
if the guest's first access to the THP isn't to its first page.

transparent_hugepage_adjust() is meant to round the faulting pfn down to
the start of the THP, but its mask is a no-op when the PMD level is
folded, and has been since THP support was added. Before 6f745f1bb5bf2
("KVM: arm64: Convert user_mem_abort() to generic page-table API"), KVM
mapped only the THP anyway, which is why the Fixes: tag is on that
commit. Kernels before f583a53c2b8a ("KVM: arm64: Make fault_ipa
immutable") round the IPA with PMD_MASK, so only the pfn line of the fix
applies there.

Tested on QEMU with a 64K, 42-bit VA host kernel, where the new
selftest scenario fails without the fix and passes with it.

Based on Linux 7.4-rc3 (aebb727447640).

Cheers,
/fuad

Fuad Tabba (2):
  KVM: arm64: Fix THP block mapping when the PMD level is folded
  KVM: arm64: selftests: Test a THP whose first fault is on a tail page

 arch/arm64/kvm/mmu.c                          |  4 +-
 .../kvm/arm64/stage2_block_transitions.c      | 56 ++++++++++++++++++-
 2 files changed, 55 insertions(+), 5 deletions(-)

[PATCH 2/2] KVM: arm64: selftests: Test a THP whose first fault is on a tail page
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

On a host whose PMD level is folded, KVM mapped host memory past a THP
when the guest first touched the THP at a page other than its first.

Add a scenario for that case. The guest writes to the second host page
of a memslot backed by one THP, then reads the first address past the
memslot. The test checks that the write landed in that page, and that
the read exits to userspace as MMIO. On a host whose PMD level isn't
folded, the scenario passes with or without the fix. It doesn't use
hugetlb pages, so it runs before the probe that skips the rest of the
test when they aren't reserved.

Signed-off-by: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

 .../kvm/arm64/stage2_block_transitions.c      | 56 ++++++++++++++++++-
 1 file changed, 53 insertions(+), 3 deletions(-)

[PATCH 1/2] KVM: arm64: Fix THP block mapping when the PMD level is folded
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

When a THP backs guest memory, transparent_hugepage_adjust() rounds the
faulting gfn and pfn down to the THP's first page, and KVM maps the
whole THP with a PMD_SIZE block. However, the rounding uses a mask of
PTRS_PER_PMD - 1, and PTRS_PER_PMD is 1 when the PMD level is folded
(64K pages with a 42-bit VA, or 16K pages with a 36-bit VA). KVM then
maps PMD_SIZE worth of pages starting at the faulting page, and the
guest gets the host memory that follows the THP. With pKVM, the
hypervisor rejects the unaligned block, so the guest doesn't get that
memory.

Before the conversion to the generic page-table API, the unrounded pfn
went into a block descriptor, where the address bits below the block
size aren't part of the output address, so only the THP was mapped.

Round to PMD_SIZE >> PAGE_SHIFT pages instead.

Fixes: 6f745f1bb5bf2 ("KVM: arm64: Convert user_mem_abort() to generic page-table API")
Cc: stable@vger.kernel.org
Signed-off-by: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>
---
 arch/arm64/kvm/mmu.c | 4 ++--
 1 file changed, 2 insertions(+), 2 deletions(-)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

[PATCH] KVM: arm64: nv: Never invalidate less than a page for a
Author: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>

The compute_tlb_inval_range() function takes the size of a nested TLB
invalidation from the TTL hint in the TLBI operand.  A guest hypervisor
can give a 4K level-3 hint, which asks for a 4K invalidation.  On a host
with 16K or 64K pages that is less than one page.  s2_mmu_unmap_ipa()
passes the 4K size to kvm_stage2_unmap_range(), which hits
WARN_ON(size & ~PAGE_MASK).

WARNING: arch/arm64/kvm/mmu.c:407 at kvm_stage2_unmap_range+0x38/0x60, CPU#1: ipas2e1_ttl_tes/568
Call trace:
 kvm_stage2_unmap_range (arch/arm64/kvm/mmu.c:410) (P)
 s2_mmu_unmap_ipa (arch/arm64/kvm/sys_regs.c:4246)
 kvm_s2_mmu_iterate_by_vmid (arch/arm64/kvm/nested.c:770)
 handle_ipas2e1is (arch/arm64/kvm/sys_regs.c:4258)
 perform_access (arch/arm64/kvm/sys_regs.c:4917)
 kvm_handle_sys_reg (arch/arm64/kvm/sys_regs.c:5455)
 handle_exit (arch/arm64/kvm/handle_exit.c:466)
 kvm_arch_vcpu_ioctl_run (arch/arm64/kvm/arm.c:1583)
 kvm_vcpu_ioctl (virt/kvm/kvm_main.c:4595)

The shadow stage-2 is built from host pages, so invalidating less than
a page makes no sense.  Return at least PAGE_SIZE.

Fixes: d1de1576dc21 ("KVM: arm64: nv: Handle FEAT_TTL hinted TLB operations")
Cc: stable@vger.kernel.org
Signed-off-by: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>

[PATCH] KVM: arm64: nv: Re-inject a stored SError after the exit
Author: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>

KVM only has room for one pending exception or PC increment per vcpu.
If something is already pending and we try to add another through
kvm_pend_exception() or kvm_incr_pc(), it triggers a WARN().  If we add
an exception on top of an exception, the first one is lost.  If we add a
PC increment on top of an exception, the PC increment is lost and the
trapped instruction runs again.

Normally an exit handler records an exception or a PC increment, and
__kvm_adjust_pc() commits it before the next guest entry.  However, with
nested virtualization kvm_nested_sync_hwstate() can add an exception
before the exit handler runs.  It re-injects a stored SError after the
guest exits but before handle_exit(), which can pend the SError as an
exception.  The exit handler then records its own on top, for example an
HVC forwarded to the guest hypervisor, the FPAC for a failed ERETAA, or
the PC increment for a trapped instruction it has emulated.  This
triggers the WARN().

In this situation where we have two exceptions, the correct order is to
handle the exit first and then the SError.  Change the
kvm_nested_sync_hwstate() function to only store the SError.  Then
kvm_nested_flush_hwstate() uses __kvm_adjust_pc() to commit the exit
handler's exception or PC increment, re-injects the SError, and the
guest is entered.  The early re-injection was there so that a pending
SError wakes the vcpu from WFI, so kvm_arch_vcpu_runnable() now treats
a deliverable stored SError as a wakeup.

Fixes: 77ee70a07357 ("KVM: arm64: nv: Honor SError exception routing / masking")
Fixes: ce66109cec86 ("KVM: arm64: nv: Take "masked" aborts to EL2 when HCRX_EL2.TMEA is set")
Cc: stable@vger.kernel.org
Signed-off-by: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>
---
 arch/arm64/include/asm/kvm_emulate.h |  1 +
 arch/arm64/kvm/arm.c                 |  1 +
 arch/arm64/kvm/inject_fault.c        |  6 ++++++
 arch/arm64/kvm/nested.c              | 16 ++++++----------
 4 files changed, 14 insertions(+), 10 deletions(-)

[PATCH] KVM: arm64: nv: Reset MMIO state when committing a nested
Author: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>

KVM has two ways to commit a pending exception, commit_pending_events()
which also resets the MMIO emulation state, and a direct call to
__kvm_adjust_pc() which doesn't.  The EL1 to EL2 path in
kvm_inject_nested() uses __kvm_adjust_pc(), but it needs to use
commit_pending_events() instead.

The scenario is: the vcpu exits to userspace for an MMIO access and
userspace then injects a synchronous external abort or an SError using
the KVM_SET_VCPU_EVENTS ioctl.  If the vcpu is in L2 context and L1 has
asked for the exception, then kvm_inject_nested() enters the exception
straight away and vcpu->mmio_needed stays set.  On the next KVM_RUN,
kvm_handle_mmio_return() completes the access in L1's context so it
skips the first instruction of L1's exception vector and writes the
MMIO data to one of L1's registers.

Replace the __kvm_adjust_pc() with commit_pending_events() so that the
MMIO emulation state is cleared.

Reported-by: Sashiko <sashiko-bot@kernel.org>
Closes: https://lore.kernel.org/all/sashiko-outbox-221074@kernel.org/
Fixes: 9aba641b9ec2 ("KVM: arm64: nv: Respect exception routing rules for SEAs")
Fixes: 77ee70a07357 ("KVM: arm64: nv: Honor SError exception routing / masking")
Cc: stable@vger.kernel.org
Signed-off-by: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>
---

 arch/arm64/kvm/emulate-nested.c   | 2 +-
 arch/arm64/kvm/guest.c            | 2 +-
